  {
    "id": 744,
    "detail": {
      "success": true,
      "data": {
        "id": 744,
        "brand_name": "Wilom",
        "brand_suffix": null,
        "brand_shot": {
          "original": "https://d1c0x5rkl7k63i.cloudfront.net/upload/drug/brand/image/rL68vMvK6MRR01GeRbCTRBggKCDYFTQFsJCAdoUrxaUaALdGi7.jpg",
          "medium": "https://d1c0x5rkl7k63i.cloudfront.net/upload/drug/brand/image/rL68vMvK6MRR01GeRbCTRBggKCDYFTQFsJCAdoUrxaUaALdGi7.jpg",
          "small": "https://d1c0x5rkl7k63i.cloudfront.net/upload/drug/brand/image/vV57M7oUNpQf2561fNLNLtzINhyZ9tlLD7O5tHuMuQUSw9Zdmc.jpg"
        },
        "brand_status": 1,
        "content": "Omeprazole",
        "brand_info": [
          {
            "title": "Therapeutic class",
            "content": "Proton-Pump Inhibitors"
          },
          {
            "title": "Regulatory Classification",
            "content": "Rx"
          },
          {
            "title": "Indications",
            "content": "Aspiration syndromes, dyspepsia, gastroesophageal reflux disease (GERD), peptic ulcer disease (PUD) including gastric and duodenal ulcer, and Zollinger-Ellison syndrome."
          },
          {
            "title": "Dose",
            "content": "<b>Dyspepsia:</b> 20 mg daily for 2-4 wks. <b>GERD:</b> 20 mg once daily for 4 weeks followed further for 4-8 weeks if not fully healed. <b>Refractory esophagitis:</b> 40 mg once daily. Maintenance therapy after healing esophagitis: 20 mg once daily. <b>Childn:</b> 0.7-1.4 mg/kg body weight once daily up to a max dose of 40 mg for 4-12 weeks. <b>PUD:</b> 20 mg single dose or 40 mg in severe cases. Treatment continued for 4 weeks for duodenal ulcer and 8 weeks for gastric ulcer. Maintenance: 20 mg once daily may be given. <b>NSAID-associated ulceration:</b> 20 mg once daily. <b>Zollinger-Ellison syndrome:</b> Initially 60 mg once daily. Prophylaxis of acid aspiration during general anesthesia: 40 mg the evening before surgery and a further 40 mg 2-6 hrs before the procedure."
          },
          {
            "title": "Contraindications",
            "content": "Hypersensitivity to omeprazole, substituted benzimidazoles, or any other constituent of the formulation."
          },
          {
            "title": "Precautions",
            "content": "Hepatic impairment. Possibility of malignancy in patients with gastric ulcer.",
            "pregnancy_risk": null
          },
          {
            "title": "Side Effects",
            "content": "Headache, diarrhea, skin rashes, pruritus, dizziness, fatigue, constipation, nausea, vomiting, flatulence, abdominal pain, arthralgia and myalgia, urticaria, and dry mouth."
          },
          {
            "title": "Drug Interactions",
            "content": "Diazepam, phenytoin, warfarin, ketoconazole, itraconazole. Voriconazole, atazanavir."
          },
          {
            "title": "Packaging",
            "content": "Capsule 40 mg (P41.60)."
          }
        ],
        "company": {
          "id": 1450,
          "name": "New Willore Pharma Corporation"
        },
        "class": "human",
        "form_class": "3",
        "prescription_class": "Rx",
        "description": null,
        "directions": null,
        "instrument": null,
        "sensitivity": null,
        "uses": null,
        "withdrawal": null,
        "dangerous": 0,
        "storage": null,
        "paid": 0,
        "keywords": null,
        "generic_content": "Omeprazole",

        ],

  {
    "id": 1205,
    "detail": {
      "success": true,
      "data": {
        "id": 1205,
        "brand_name": "Wilomax",
        "brand_suffix": null,
        "brand_shot": {
          "original": "https://d1c0x5rkl7k63i.cloudfront.net/upload/drug/brand/image/7V4wJcKsqz6WGGSWZXFxOdm0j1dM0vjtWZPo6UzsY1PglmPgfx.jpg",
          "medium": "https://d1c0x5rkl7k63i.cloudfront.net/upload/drug/brand/image/7V4wJcKsqz6WGGSWZXFxOdm0j1dM0vjtWZPo6UzsY1PglmPgfx.jpg",
          "small": "https://d1c0x5rkl7k63i.cloudfront.net/upload/drug/brand/image/1k88f6afR3CDcrcFwMHtfwF5PdjtG6FAIPDfBJPVLoZ1gHs99n.jpg"
        },
        "brand_status": 1,
        "content": "Amlodipine (as besilate)",
        "brand_info": [
          {
            "title": "Therapeutic class",
            "content": "Calcium Channel Blockers (Antihypertensives)"
          },
          {
            "title": "Regulatory Classification",
            "content": "Rx"
          },
          {
            "title": "Indications",
            "content": "Management of hypertension and prophylaxis of angina pectoris."
          },
          {
            "title": "Dose",
            "content": "<b>HTN - Adults</b>: Initially 5 mg once daily. Max 10 mg once daily. <b>Small, fragile, or elderly patients with hepatic insufficiency</b>: Initially 2.5 mg once daily. <b>Chronic stable or vasospastic angina - Adults</b>: 5-10 mg. Elderly and patients with hepatic insufficiency: Lower doses."
          },
          {
            "title": "Contraindications",
            "content": "Hypersensitivity. Patients suffering from cardiogenic shock, unstable angina, significant aortic stenosis, and obesity."
          },
          {
            "title": "Precautions",
            "content": "Patients with hypotension, poor cardiac reserve, heart failure, severe aortic stenosis. Should not be used for cardiogenic shock, in patients who recently suffered MI, acute unstable angina, anginal attack in chronic stable angina. Ischemic pain. Hepatic impairment. Pregnancy and lactation. <b><a href=\"#\" id=\"has_preg_risk\">Pregnancy Risk</a>:</b> Category C.",
            "pregnancy_risk": {
              "header": "Pregnancy Risk Category by the US FDA:",
              "categories": [
                {
                  "title": "A",
                  "content": "Adequate and compelling studies in humans do not show any evidence that the product poses a risk to the baby in the womb."
                },
                {
                  "title": "B",
                  "content": "Adequate and compelling studies in animals do not show a risk to the baby, but there are not enough compelling studies in humans. Otherwise, animal studies show a risk but studies on humans do not show any."
                },
                {
                  "title": "C",
                  "content": "Adequate and compelling studies in animals show a risk to the baby, but there are not enough compelling studies in humans. However, the product may be used in pregnant women in certain situations despite potential risks."
                },
                {
                  "title": "D",
                  "content": "Adequate and compelling studies in humans show a risk to the baby. However, the product may be used in pregnant women in certain situations despite the potential risks."
                },
                {
                  "title": "X",
                  "content": "Adequate and compelling studies in animals or humans show a risk to the baby in the womb. Also, use or experience in humans shows a risk. The danger of using the product in pregnant women clearly outweighs its benefits."
                }
              ]
            }
          },
          {
            "title": "Side Effects",
            "content": "Abdominal pain, nausea, palpitations, flushing, edema, headache, dizziness, sleep disturbances, fatigue, GI disturbances, dry mouth, taste disturbances, hypotension, syncope, chest pain, dyspnea, mood changes, tremor, paresthesia, urinary disturbances, impotence, gynecomastia, weight change, myalgia, visual disturbances, pruritus, rashes (including erythema multiforme), alopecia, purpura, pancreatitis, hepatitis, jaundice, cholestasis, gingival hyperplasia, MI, arrhythmia, vasculitis, hyperglycemia, thrombocytopenia, angioedema and urticaria."
          },
          {
            "title": "Drug Interactions",
            "content": "Beta-blockers, antidiabetics."
          },
          {
            "title": "Packaging",
            "content": "Tablet 5 mg (P9.80), 10 mg (P9.80)."
          }
        ],
        "company": {
          "id": 1450,
          "name": "New Willore Pharma Corporation"
        },
        "class": "human",
        "form_class": "3",
        "prescription_class": "Rx",
        "description": null,
        "directions": null,
        "instrument": null,
        "sensitivity": null,

  {
    "id": 910,
    "detail": {

      "data": {
        "id": 910,

        "brand_shot": {

          "medium": "https://d1c0x5rkl7k63i.cloudfront.net/upload/drug/brand/image/zAFd29OPbOsI9ldRvf3Xwtvh9sMXkCZ0IvSkRJvpSs7pqQUc9D.jpg",
          "small": "https://d1c0x5rkl7k63i.cloudfront.net/upload/drug/brand/image/4pVxBxxH0MahBDFGxMe3AogOnH4SEe4j07jSyivxtBG4ymAlHw.jpg"
        },
        "brand_status": 1,
        "content": "Losartan potassium",
        "brand_info": [
          {
            "title": "Therapeutic class",
            "content": "Angiotensin II Receptor Blockers"
          },
          {
            "title": "Regulatory Classification",
            "content": "Rx"
          },
          {
            "title": "Indications",
            "content": "Management of hypertension. Treatment of diabetic nephropathy."
          },
          {
            "title": "Dose",
            "content": "50 mg once daily, may be increased to 100 mg as a single dose or in 2 divided doses. <b>Elderly >75 yrs and patients with moderate to severe renal impairment (CrCl < 20 mL/min) or intravascular fluid depletion:</b> Initially 25 mg once daily. Hepatic impairment: Reduce dose."
          },
          {
            "title": "Contraindications",
            "content": "Pregnancy."
          },
          {
            "title": "Precautions",
            "content": "Patients with renal artery stenosis, renal, or hepatic impairment. Pregnancy and lactation. <b><a href=\"#\" id=\"has_preg_risk\">Pregnancy Risk</a>:</b> Category C but D in 2<sup>nd</sup> and 3<sup>rd</sup> trimesters.",
            "pregnancy_risk": {
              "header": "Pregnancy Risk Category by the US FDA:",
              "categories": [
                {
                  "title": "A",
                  "content": "Adequate and compelling studies in humans do not show any evidence that the product poses a risk to the baby in the womb."
                },
                {
                  "title": "B",
                  "content": "Adequate and compelling studies in animals do not show a risk to the baby, but there are not enough compelling studies in humans. Otherwise, animal studies show a risk but studies on humans do not show any."
                },
                {
                  "title": "C",
                  "content": "Adequate and compelling studies in animals show a risk to the baby, but there are not enough compelling studies in humans. However, the product may be used in pregnant women in certain situations despite potential risks."
                },
                {
                  "title": "D",
                  "content": "Adequate and compelling studies in humans show a risk to the baby. However, the product may be used in pregnant women in certain situations despite the potential risks."
                },
                {
                  "title": "X",
                  "content": "Adequate and compelling studies in animals or humans show a risk to the baby in the womb. Also, use or experience in humans shows a risk. The danger of using the product in pregnant women clearly outweighs its benefits."
                }
              ]
            }
          },
          {
            "title": "Side Effects",
            "content": "Dizziness and dose-related hypotension. Hyperkalemia in patients with renal disease. Respiratory tract disorder, back pain, GI disturbances, fatigue, and neutropenia."
          },
          {
            "title": "Drug Interactions",
            "content": "<b>Other antihypertensives.</b> Potassium supplements, potassium-sparing diuretics, other drugs that can cause hyperkalemia. NSAIDs."
          },
          {
            "title": "Packaging",
            "content": "Film-coated tablet 50 mg (P13.80), 100 mg (P18.00)."
          }
        ],
        "company": {
          "id": 1450,
          "name": "New Willore Pharma Corporation"
        },

  {
    "id": 1060,
    "detail": {
      "success": true,
      "data": {
        "id": 1060,
        "brand_name": "Wilopres Plus",
        "brand_suffix": null,
        "brand_shot": {
          "original": "https://d1c0x5rkl7k63i.cloudfront.net/upload/drug/brand/image/y7Y8gCVnp7udtiUo8vRLk8GRAD3bImxsOlqEa3V4dtZfvyiQTb.jpg",
          "medium": "https://d1c0x5rkl7k63i.cloudfront.net/upload/drug/brand/image/y7Y8gCVnp7udtiUo8vRLk8GRAD3bImxsOlqEa3V4dtZfvyiQTb.jpg",
          "small": "https://d1c0x5rkl7k63i.cloudfront.net/upload/drug/brand/image/iSlswvfGYeCuCMzV46IcyibBY4Z12uwL3RU9lZvoRIRWhGPl0S.jpg"
        },
        "brand_status": 1,
        "content": "Losartan potassium, hydrochlorothiazide",
        "brand_info": [
          {
            "title": "Therapeutic class",
            "content": "Angiotensin II Receptor Blockers"
          },
          {
            "title": "Regulatory Classification",
            "content": "Rx"
          },
          {
            "title": "Indications",
            "content": "Treatment of hypertension (HTN). To reduce risk of stroke in patient with hypertension and ventricular hypertrophy."
          },
          {
            "title": "Dose",
            "content": "<b>HTN:</b> 50 mg/12.5 mg once daily, may be increased after 3 weeks to 100 mg/25 mg once daily. <b>Patients with HTN and left ventricular hypertrophy:</b> Initially 50 mg/12.5 mg once daily. May be increased to 100 mg/25 mg once daily, if needed."
          },
          {
            "title": "Contraindications",
            "content": "Hypersensitivity. Patients with anuria; diabetes or renal impairment (GFR < 60 mL/min) who are taking aliskiren. Pregnancy."
          },
          {
            "title": "Precautions",
            "content": "Patients with intravascular vol depletion. Monitor serum electrolytes periodically. May alter glucose tolerance; uric acid, cholesterol and triglyceride serum levels. Patients with renal artery stenosis, CKD, severe CHF; risk factors for acute angle-closure glaucoma; SLE. Risk of non-melanoma skin CA. Limit exposure to sunlight and UV rays. Lactation. <b><a href=\"#\" id=\"has_preg_risk\">Pregnancy Risk</a>:</b> Category C but D in 2<sup>nd</sup> and 3<sup>rd</sup> trimesters.",
            "pregnancy_risk": {
              "header": "Pregnancy Risk Category by the US FDA:",
              "categories": [
                {
                  "title": "A",
                  "content": "Adequate and compelling studies in humans do not show any evidence that the product poses a risk to the baby in the womb."
                },
                {
                  "title": "B",
                  "content": "Adequate and compelling studies in animals do not show a risk to the baby, but there are not enough compelling studies in humans. Otherwise, animal studies show a risk but studies on humans do not show any."
                },
                {
                  "title": "C",
                  "content": "Adequate and compelling studies in animals show a risk to the baby, but there are not enough compelling studies in humans. However, the product may be used in pregnant women in certain situations despite potential risks."
                },
                {
                  "title": "D",
                  "content": "Adequate and compelling studies in humans show a risk to the baby. However, the product may be used in pregnant women in certain situations despite the potential risks."
                },
                {
                  "title": "X",
                  "content": "Adequate and compelling studies in animals or humans show a risk to the baby in the womb. Also, use or experience in humans shows a risk. The danger of using the product in pregnant women clearly outweighs its benefits."
                }
              ]
            }
          },
          {
            "title": "Side Effects",
            "content": "Changes in renal function. Dose-related orthostatic effects, necrotizing angiitis (vasculitis, cutaneous vasculitis); anorexia, hyperglycemia, hyperuricemia, electrolyte imbalance including hyponatremia and hypokalemia; xanthopsia, transient blurred vision. Rash, pruritus, purpura, toxic epidermal necrolysis, urticaria, photosensitivity, cutaneous lupus erythematosus. Chest pain, malaise, weakness."
          },
          {
            "title": "Drug Interactions",
            "content": "Drugs that cause hyperkalemia, lithium, NSAIDs including COX2 inhibitors; ACE inhibitors, antidiabetic drugs, cholestyramine, colestipol resins; alcohol, barbiturates, or narcotics. Other antihypertensives. Skeletal muscle relaxants (e.g. tubocurarine)."
          },
          {
            "title": "Packaging",
            "content": "Film-coated tablet 50 mg/12.5 mg (P17.80), 100 mg/25 mg (P20.80)."
          }
        ],
        "company": {
          "id": 1450,
          "name": "New Willore Pharma Corporation"
        },

  {
    "id": 1847,
    "detail": {
      "success": true,
      "data": {
        "id": 1847,
        "brand_name": "Wilovast",
        "brand_suffix": null,
        "brand_shot": {
          "original": "https://d1c0x5rkl7k63i.cloudfront.net/upload/drug/brand/image/VQL1JKk0Ml9hZPSAEWL0WYD9l1k74FCtqHi6AhjlSlWaWKpZ33.jpg",
          "medium": "https://d1c0x5rkl7k63i.cloudfront.net/upload/drug/brand/image/mmH7C2FhMoZmX55PfgCE13O4FCNwuVAAacLJtSzywrWmTVq1MS.jpg",
          "small": "https://d1c0x5rkl7k63i.cloudfront.net/upload/drug/brand/image/XpgYeC3cEfOTKpoOLVU6aaFhN7LdJdbMuZ6NcNgUqRSVTWNs9N.jpg"
        },
        "brand_status": 1,
        "content": "Rosuvastatin (as calcium)",
        "brand_info": [
          {
            "title": "Therapeutic class",
            "content": "Statins"
          },
          {
            "title": "Regulatory Classification",
            "content": "Rx"
          },
          {
            "title": "Indications",
            "content": "Management of hyperlipidemias, including primary hypercholesterolemia (type IIa), mixed dyslipidemia (type IIb), and hypertriglyceridemia (type IV). Homozygous familial hypercholesterolemia."
          },
          {
            "title": "Dose",
            "content": "Initially 5-10 mg in the evening. Doses may be adjusted at intervals of not less than 4 weeks up to max 80 mg daily in 3 divided doses of 20 mg, 20 mg, and an evening dose of 40 mg. <b>Patients with ischemic heart disease:</b> Initially 20 mg. <b>Patients taking ciclosporin, fibric acid derivatives, or nicotinic acid, and when risk of myopathy must be considered:</b> 10 mg once daily."
          },
          {
            "title": "Contraindications",
            "content": "Hypersensitivity. Active liver disease or unexplained persistent elevation of serum transaminases. Pregnancy and lactation."
          },
          {
            "title": "Precautions",
            "content": "History of liver disease and/or consumption of substantial quantities of alcohol. Predisposing factors for myopathy, e.g. renal impairment, advanced age and inadequately treated hypothyroidism. Discontinue therapy if with markedly elevated CK levels or if myopathy is suspected.  Acute conditions suggestive of myopathy or predisposing to development of renal failure secondary to rhabdomyolysis. Concomitant use of gemfibrozil, ciclosporin, other lipid-lowering agents, lopinavir/ritonavir.",
            "pregnancy_risk": null
          },
          {
            "title": "Side Effects",
            "content": "GI disturbances, headache, skin rashes, dizziness, blurred vision, insomnia, dysgeusia."
          },
          {
            "title": "Drug Interactions",
            "content": "Ciclosporin, fibric acid derivatives (gemfibrozil), azole antifungals, sofosbuvir/velpatasvir/voxilaprevir, macrolides, nicotinic acid."
          },
          {
            "title": "Packaging",
            "content": "Film-coated tablet 10 mg (P20.80)."
          }
        ],
        "company": {
          "id": 1450,
          "name": "New Willore Pharma Corporation"
        },

          {

            "content": "Treatment of respiratory tract infections (including otitis media), skin and soft tissue infections, and uncomplicated genital infections. May be used for prophylaxis and as a component in the treatment of <i>Mycobacterium avium</i> complex (MAC) infections. Management of trachoma and typhoid."
          },
          {
            "title": "Dose",
            "content": "<b>Adults: </b>500 mg as a single dose for 3 days. <b>Uncomplicated genital infections due to <i>Chlamydia trachomatis</i></b>: 1 g as a single dose. <b>Uncomplicated gonorrhea</b>: 2 g as a single dose. <b>Prophylaxis of disseminated MAC infections</b>: 1.2 g once weekly."
          },
          {
            "title": "Contraindications",
            "content": "Hypersensitivity to azithromycin, erythromycin, or other macrolides."
          },
          {
            "title": "Precautions",
            "content": "Patients with significant hepatic disease. Concomitant use of ergot derivatives. Pregnancy and lactation.",
            "pregnancy_risk": null
          },
          {
            "title": "Side Effects",
            "content": "GI disturbances, headache, somnolence, and taste disturbances."
          },
          {
            "title": "Drug Interactions",
            "content": "Antacids containing aluminum or magnesium, ergot derivatives, digoxin, rifabutin."
          },

  {
    "id": 4678,
    "detail": {
      "success": true,
      "data": {
        "id": 4678,
        "brand_name": "Xagulant",
        "brand_suffix": null,
        "brand_shot": {
          "original": "https://d1c0x5rkl7k63i.cloudfront.net/upload/drug/brand/image/awB7WeknyYGXsheLKzYem6kRsT68912UQga7n0ZUYNldn0p3Gk.jpg",
          "medium": "https://d1c0x5rkl7k63i.cloudfront.net/upload/drug/brand/image/jmHf7YGucOMY8dvXBKF8zxjoyQz9ivRZrGUvjkf3PDFZR3AYCn.jpg",
          "small": "https://d1c0x5rkl7k63i.cloudfront.net/upload/drug/brand/image/jwYsfii5bu3D12NwZ8RvatmoDwJ9NRJqIOtqnwDqVh4uMR59bd.jpg"
        },
        "brand_status": 1,
        "content": "Apixaban",
        "brand_info": [
          {
            "title": "Therapeutic class",
            "content": "Anticoagulants"
          },
          {
            "title": "Regulatory Classification",
            "content": "Rx"
          },
          {
            "title": "Indications",
            "content": "Prevention of stroke and systemic embolism in adult patients with non-valvular atrial fibrillation (NVAF), with one or more risk factors, such as prior stroke or transient ischemic attack (TIA); age &ge;75 yrs; hypertension; diabetes mellitus; symptomatic heart failure (NYHA Class &ge; II). Treatment of deep vein thrombosis (DVT) and pulmonary embolism (PE). Prevention of recurrent DVT and PE in adults."
          },
          {
            "title": "Dose",
            "content": "<b>Prevention of stroke and systemic embolism – NVAF</b>: 5 mg twice daily. In patients with at least 2 of the following characteristics: age &ge;80 yrs, BW &le;60 kg, or serum creatinine &ge;1.5 mg/dL (133 micromole/L): 2.5 mg twice daily. <b>Treatment of DVT and PE</b>: 10 mg twice daily for 7 days, followed by 5 mg twice daily. <b>Prevention of recurrent DVT and PE</b>: 2.5 mg twice daily after at least 6 months of treatment for DVT or PE. <b>Cardioversion</b> - For patients initiating treatment with apixaban: 5 single doses of 5 mg twice daily before cardioversion. Dose reduction to 2.5 mg twice daily. If cardioversion is required before 5 doses of apixaban can be administered, give 10 mg loading dose then 5 mg twice daily. Dose reduction to 5 mg loading dose followed by 2.5 mg twice. Loading dose to be given at least 2 hrs before cardioversion."
          },
          {
            "title": "Contraindications",
            "content": "Hypersensitivity. Clinically significant active bleeding. Hepatic disease associated with coagulopathy. GI ulceration, presence of malignant neoplasms at high risk of bleeding, recent brain or spinal injury, ophthalmic surgery, intracranial hemorrhage, known or suspected esophageal varices, arteriovenous malformations, vascular aneurysms, major intraspinal, intracerebral vascular abnormalities."
          },
          {
            "title": "Precautions",
            "content": "Increased risk of hemorrhage. Patients with prosthetic heart valves, antiphospholipid syndrome, active cancer; elevated liver enzymes alanine aminotransferase (ALT)/aspartate aminotransferase; CrCl < 15 mL/min and those undergoing dialysis; mild or moderate hepatic impairment (Child-Pugh A or B); severe renal impairment (CrCl 15-29 mL/min).  For surgery and invasive procedures, discontinue at least 48 hrs prior to elective surgery or invasive procedures with a moderate or high risk of bleeding and at least 24 hrs prior with a low risk of bleeding. Restart after procedure as soon as possible provided that adequate hemostasis has been established. Pregnancy and lactation.",
            "pregnancy_risk": null
          },
          {
            "title": "Side Effects",
            "content": "Hemorrhage, contusion, epistaxis, and hematoma. Anemia, thrombocytopenia, hypotension, skin rash, hematuria."
          },
          {
            "title": "Drug Interactions",
            "content": "Anticoagulants. Strong CYP3A4 and P-gp inhibitors (e.g. ketoconazole), inducers (e.g. rifampin), and HIV protease inhibitors (e.g. ritonavir). Platelet aggregation inhibitors and NSAIDs including aspirin."
          },
          {
            "title": "Packaging",
            "content": "Film-coated tablet 2.5 mg (P67.00), 5 mg (P67.00)."
          }
        ],

  {
    "id": 1057,
    "detail": {
      "success": true,
      "data": {
        "id": 1057,
        "brand_name": "Xamiol",
        "brand_suffix": null,
        "brand_shot": {
          "original": null,
          "medium": null,
          "small": null
        },
        "brand_status": 1,
        "content": "Calcipotriol monohydrate, betamethasone dipropionate",
        "brand_info": [
          {
            "title": "Therapeutic class",
            "content": "Cell Suppressants"
          },
          {
            "title": "Regulatory Classification",
            "content": "Rx"
          },
          {
            "title": "Indications",
            "content": "Topical treatment of scalp psoriasis."
          },
          {
            "title": "Dose",
            "content": "Apply 1-4 g (4 g corresponds to 1 tsp) to the affected scalp areas once daily. Recommended treatment period: 4 weeks. Max daily dose: 15 g. Max weekly dose: 100 g. Max treated area: 30% of BSA. Shake bottle well before use. Gel should remain on scalp during the night or day."
          },
          {
            "title": "Contraindications",
            "content": "Hypersensitivity. Patients with known disorders of calcium metabolism, viral lesions of the skin, fungal or bacterial skin infections, parasitic infections, skin manifestations in relation to TB, perioral dermatitis, atrophic skin, striae atrophicae, fragility of skin veins, ichthyosis, acne vulgaris, acne rosacea, rosacea, ulcers and wounds. Erythrodermic, exfoliative and pustular psoriasis."
          },
          {
            "title": "Precautions",
            "content": "Avoid application on the face, mouth, eyes and genitals, on large areas of damaged skin, or on mucous membranes or in skin folds and under occlusive dressings. Hypercalcemia if max weekly dose exceeded. Concurrent treatment with other topical anti-psoriatic products administered systemically or with phototherapy. Discontinue if infection worsens. Avoid long-term use. Limit or avoid excessive exposure to natural or artificial sunlight. May cause local skin reactions (e.g. contact dermatitis), or irritation to the eyes and mucous membranes. Severe renal and hepatic impairment. Pregnancy and lactation.",
            "pregnancy_risk": null
          },
          {
            "title": "Side Effects",
            "content": "Pruritus. Skin infection, folliculitis; eye irritation; exacerbations, dermatitis, erythema, rash, acne, skin burning sensation, skin irritation, dry skin; application site pain."
          },
          {
            "title": "Packaging",
            "content": "Gel 50 mcg/500 mcg per g x 15 g."
          }
        ],
        "company": {
          "id": 3866,
          "name": "DKSH Market Expansion Services Philippines, Inc."
        },

        "brand_shot": {

          "medium": "https://d1c0x5rkl7k63i.cloudfront.net/upload/drug/brand/image/4B7nWv0RJ0uIzHY8WHAW58ig7Wekk4hugp9mDImUWXpJ7ixQqC.jpg",
          "small": "https://d1c0x5rkl7k63i.cloudfront.net/upload/drug/brand/image/jxJ1D4IdcdVlJMSHppX4u28sD9aEJspw9SEz0DHTMaWQ05SKYO.jpg"
        },
        "brand_status": 1,
        "content": "Alprazolam",
        "brand_info": [
          {
            "title": "Therapeutic class",
            "content": "Anxiolytics"
          },
          {
            "title": "Regulatory Classification",
            "content": "Rx, Exempt Dangerous Drug"
          },
          {
            "title": "Indications",
            "content": "Treatment of anxiety, depression, and mixed-anxiety depression either associated or not associated with other functional or organic disease; panic disorders.er functional or organic disease, panic disorders."
          },
          {
            "title": "Dose",
            "content": "<b>Anxiety</b>: 0.75-1.5 mg daily in divided doses. Usual dose range: 0.5-4 mg daily in divided dose. <b>Depression</b>: 1.5 mg daily in divided doses. Usual dose range: 1.5-4.5 mg daily in divided doses. <b>Panic disorders</b>: 0.5-1 mg at bedtime or 0.5 mg three times daily, adjust dose in increments of not &gt;1 mg/day every 3-4 days. <b>Elderly</b>: 0.5-0.75 mg daily in divided doses, gradually increased if needed and tolerated."
          },
          {
            "title": "Contraindications",
            "content": "Hypersensitivity to alprazolam or other benzodiazepines."
          },
          {
            "title": "Precautions",
            "content": "Concomitant use of opioids. Patients with impaired renal or hepatic functions; history of alcoholism or drug abuse; severely depressed or suicidal ideation or plans; panic disorders. Risk for habituation and emotional/physical dependence; episodes of hypomania and mania. Withdrawal symptoms following rapid decrease or abrupt discontinuance. May cause inability to drive and use machinery. Pregnancy and lactation.",
            "pregnancy_risk": null
          },
          {
            "title": "Side Effects",
            "content": "Sedation, somnolence, ataxia, memory impairment, dysarthria, dizziness, headache; constipation, dry mouth; fatigue irritability."
          },
          {
            "title": "Drug Interactions",
            "content": "Opioids, alcohol, other CNS depression producing drugs; azole-type antifungals (e.g. ketoconazole, itraconazole); nefazodone, fluvoxamine, cimetidine; fluoxetine, propoxyphene, OCP, diltiazem, macrolide antibiotics (e.g. erythromycin, troleandomycin), HIV protease inhibitors (e.g. ritonavir); digoxin."
          },
          {
            "title": "Packaging",
            "content": "Tablet 250 mcg (P20.92), 500 mcg (P30.15), 1 mg (P51.71)."
          }
        ],
        "company": {
          "id": 3917,
          "name": "Viatris, Inc."

    "id": 2915,
    "detail": {
      "success": true,
      "data": {
        "id": 2915,
        "brand_name": "Xelevia",
        "brand_suffix": null,
        "brand_shot": {
          "original": "https://d1c0x5rkl7k63i.cloudfront.net/upload/drug/brand/image/w5GrabMSZD1WvhFHUemXIpEVPctd3bbyMRr8ykehLUoqOjfwjr.jpg",
          "medium": "https://d1c0x5rkl7k63i.cloudfront.net/upload/drug/brand/image/FBzojOz4OZ7ix1TNAqpaRR4p3XJCwDIjmKcPhNBqr0Oewc7ZB1.jpg",
          "small": "https://d1c0x5rkl7k63i.cloudfront.net/upload/drug/brand/image/WmbGtEFn6Ea65QW14IsrQpOQydsgqAMl1iDAig8eEVxovwWgAh.jpg"
        },
        "brand_status": 1,
        "content": "Sitagliptin",
        "brand_info": [
          {
            "title": "Therapeutic class",
            "content": "Dipeptidyl Peptidase-4 (DPP-4) Inhibitors"
          },
          {
            "title": "Regulatory Classification",
            "content": "Rx"
          },
          {
            "title": "Indications",
            "content": "Monotherapy: Adjunct to diet and exercise to improve glycemic control in patients with type 2 diabetes mellitus (DM). Combination therapy: With metformin, a sulfonylurea, or a PPAR-gamma agonist (e.g. thiazolidinediones) as initial therapy or when the single agents alone, with diet and exercise, does not provide adequate glycemic control; With metformin and a sulfonylurea, or a PPAR-gamma agonist (e.g. thiazolidinediones) when dual therapy with these agents, with diet and exercise, does not provide adequate glycemic control; With insulin as an adjunct to diet and exercise to improve glycemic control in patients with type 2 DM."
          },
          {
            "title": "Dose",
            "content": "100 mg once daily as monotherapy or as combination therapy with metformin, a sulfonylurea, insulin (with or without metformin), a PPAR-gamma agonist (e.g. thiazolidinediones), metformin plus a sulfonylurea, or metformin plus a PPAR-gamma agonist. A lower dose of sulfonylurea or insulin may be considered to reduce risk of hypoglycemia. <b>Renal impairment</b> - eGFR &ge;30-&lt;45 mL/min/1.73 m<sup>2</sup>: 50 mg once daily; &ge;15-&lt;30 mL/min/1.73 m<sup>2</sup> or with ESRD (eGFR &lt;15 mL/min/1.73 m<sup>2</sup>) requiring hemodialysis or peritoneal dialysis: 25 mg once daily."
          },
          {
            "title": "Contraindications",
            "content": "Hypersensitivity."
          },
          {
            "title": "Precautions",
            "content": "Type 1 DM, treatment of diabetic ketoacidosis, acute pancreatitis. Patients with renal impairment. Hypoglycemia in combination with sulfonylurea or with insulin. Hypersensitivity reactions. Bullous pemphigoid. Elderly. Childn &lt;18 yrs. Pregnancy and lactation.",
            "pregnancy_risk": null
          },
          {
            "title": "Side Effects",
            "content": "Hypoglycemia, abdominal pain, nausea, vomiting, diarrhea, headache, URTI, cough, fungal skin infection, peripheral edema, dyspepsia, flatulence, influenza, acute pancreatitis, hypersensitivity reactions, worsening renal function, nasopharyngitis, constipation, arthralgia, myalgia, pain in extremity, back pain, pruritus."
          },
          {
            "title": "Drug Interactions",
            "content": "Digoxin."
          },
          {
            "title": "Packaging",
            "content": "Film-coated tablet 50 mg (P34.68), 100 mg (P35.48)."
          }
        ],
        "company": {
          "id": 93,
          "name": "Natrapharm, Inc."
        },

  {
    "id": 4311,
    "detail": {
      "success": true,
      "data": {
        "id": 4311,
        "brand_name": "Xenazole",
        "brand_suffix": null,
        "brand_shot": {
          "original": "https://d1c0x5rkl7k63i.cloudfront.net/upload/drug/brand/image/cqaBA4SX4awiQQNGD0FzahDVxAgURT55uTGwwScbpzGoOtYqoz.jpg",
          "medium": "https://d1c0x5rkl7k63i.cloudfront.net/upload/drug/brand/image/CboaqxmbP9XzfXiaZYlbPBHPOKy05dO4vH6IW3LLXr02DXa9Tk.jpg",
          "small": "https://d1c0x5rkl7k63i.cloudfront.net/upload/drug/brand/image/oOtD3FvdHVrv22poiwHYjcI3KIBOm4BQWHy5g9TdiDC43BAwSC.jpg"
        },
        "brand_status": 1,
        "content": "Letrozole",
        "brand_info": [
          {
            "title": "Therapeutic class",
            "content": "Hormones and Antagonists in Chemotherapy"
          },
          {
            "title": "Regulatory Classification",
            "content": "Rx"
          },
          {
            "title": "Indications",
            "content": "Adjuvant treatment of postmenopausal women with HR-positive early breast cancer and extended adjuvant treatment of hormone-dependent early breast cancer in postmenopausal women who have received prior standard adjuvant tamoxifen therapy for 5 yrs. Treatment of advanced breast cancer in women with natural or artificially induced postmenopausal status after relapse or disease progression, who have previously been treated with antiestrogens. 1<sup>st</sup> line treatment in postmenopausal women with hormone-dependent advanced breast cancer."
          },
          {
            "title": "Dose",
            "content": "<b>Adults (including elderly)</b>: 2.5 mg once daily. <b>As adjuvant treatment – Duration</b>: 5 yrs or until tumor relapse occurs. <b>For patients with advanced or metastatic disease or as extended adjuvant treatment</b>: Continue treatment until tumor progression is evident."
          },
          {
            "title": "Contraindications",
            "content": "Hypersensitivity. Premenopausal women. Pregnancy and lactation."
          },
          {
            "title": "Precautions",
            "content": "Patients with CrCl &lt;10 mL/min. Patients with severe hepatic impairment (Child-Pugh score C). Increased risk for osteoporosis and/or bone fractures. Sexually active females of reproductive potential should use effective contraception during and until 20 days after stopping treatment. Only women of confirmed postmenopausal endocrine status should receive letrozole. May impair fertility in males of reproductive potential. May impair ability to drive or operate machinery.",
            "pregnancy_risk": null
          },
          {
            "title": "Side Effects",
            "content": "Hypercholesterolemia, hot flush, hyperhidrosis, arthralgia, fatigue (including asthenia, malaise), increased or decreased appetite, depression, headache, dizziness, vertigo, palpitations, HTN, nausea, vomiting, dyspepsia, constipation, diarrhea, abdominal pain, alopecia, dry skin, rash (including erythematous, maculopapular, psoriasiform, and vesicular rash), myalgia, bone pain, osteoporosis, bone fractures, arthritis, back pain, vaginal hemorrhage, peripheral edema, chest pain, increased weight, and fall."
          },
          {
            "title": "Drug Interactions",
            "content": "Other anti-estrogens or estrogen-containing products. Strong CYP3A4 inhibitors e.g. ketoconazole, itraconazole, voriconazole, ritonavir, clarithromycin, and telithromycin. CYP2A6 inhibitors e.g. methoxsalen. CYP3A4 inducers e.g. phenytoin, rifampicin, carbamazepine, phenobarbital, and St. John’s wort. Tamoxifen. Clopidogrel"
          },
          {
            "title": "Packaging",
            "content": "Film-coated tablet 2.5 mg (P266.00)."
          }
        ],
        "company": {
          "id": 3399,
          "name": "Bio-Onco, Inc."

          "small": "https://d1c0x5rkl7k63i.cloudfront.net/upload/drug/brand/image/xgK4HWfJWI4w2hyY0jwo3Cklv5asMy2BR1YzqYONANSCjS1UcZ.jpg"
        },
        "brand_status": 1,
        "content": "Orlistat",
        "brand_info": [
          {
            "title": "Therapeutic class",
            "content": "Anti-Obesity Agents"
          },
          {
            "title": "Regulatory Classification",
            "content": "Non-Rx"
          },
          {
            "title": "Indications",
            "content": "Treatment of obese or overweight patients with associated comorbidities in conjunction with a mildly hypocaloric diet."
          },
          {
            "title": "Dose",
            "content": "1 cap during or up to 1 hr after each main meal. Dose may be skipped if the meal is missed or contains no fat."
          },
          {
            "title": "Contraindications",
            "content": "Hypersensitivity. Chronic malabsorption syndrome. Cholestasis. Lactation."
          },
          {
            "title": "Precautions",
            "content": "Patients with renal and hepatic impairment. Patients should be on a nutritionally balanced meal, mildly hypocaloric diet that contains approx 30% of calories from fat. Distribute daily intake of fat over 3 main meals. GI events may increase when taken with a high-fat diet. May require dose reduction of hypoglycemic drugs. Use of multivitamins should be considered to ensure adequate nutrition. Coagulation parameters e.g. INR should be monitored in patients concomitantly treated with oral anticoagulants. Childn < 12 yrs. Pregnancy.",
            "pregnancy_risk": null
          },
          {
            "title": "Side Effects",
            "content": "Hypoglycemia, abdominal distention. Oily spotting, flatus with discharge, fecal urgency, fatty/oily stool, oily evacuation, increased defecation, fecal incontinence. Abdominal pain/discomfort, flatulence, tooth disorder, gingival disorder. Weight loss."
          },
          {
            "title": "Drug Interactions",
            "content": "Vit D, E, b-carotene, cyclosporin, amiodarone, antiepileptics."
          },
          {
            "title": "Packaging",
            "content": "Capsule 120 mg (P49.50)."
          }
        ],

    "detail": {
      "success": true,
      "data": {
        "id": 3634,
        "brand_name": "Xerthra PRP Kit",
        "brand_suffix": null,
        "brand_shot": {
          "original": "https://d1c0x5rkl7k63i.cloudfront.net/upload/drug/brand/image/Qe35yJAWmuR5VYvV74lnpqFWBHllXANc2oYAmMoRBQtGk8Iep4.jpg",
          "medium": "https://d1c0x5rkl7k63i.cloudfront.net/upload/drug/brand/image/HR0tj3ZdMKnob5AcNBF9wuVjlf5w8m62yPZgpw19aE2jdBlLpV.jpg",
          "small": "https://d1c0x5rkl7k63i.cloudfront.net/upload/drug/brand/image/SY1OYW3WqX3B0C3NlQdi5jh0LK9lsjkPFiZcySJ1Huq3lnni8P.jpg"
        },
        "brand_status": 1,
        "content": null,
        "brand_info": [
          {
            "title": "Description",
            "content": "A procedure pack for isolation and separation of platelet-rich-plasma (PRP) from patient's own peripheral blood."
          },
          {
            "title": "Uses",
            "content": "To separate drawn blood into RBC and plasma using a centrifugal separator. For use in inflammatory phase of disease when regenerative action of growth factors as well as regulatory action of leukocytes are required. May also be used in aesthetic medicine, dentistry, dermatology, and sports medicine."
          },
          {
            "title": "Direction",
            "content": "The preparation is made up to 15 mL of the patient's venous blood. After taking the blood, it is spun in a special tube. The quality of the plasma-rich blood depends on the separating device used and the appropriate centrifuge. Then, the prepared PRP is administered by a physician to the affected area."
          },
          {
            "title": "Packaging",
            "content": "PRP kit."
          }
        ],
        "company": {
          "id": 1835,
          "name": "JCS Pharmaceuticals, Inc."
        },

  {
    "id": 3565,
    "detail": {
      "success": true,
      "data": {
        "id": 3565,
        "brand_name": "Xet 20",
        "brand_suffix": null,
        "brand_shot": {
          "original": "https://d1c0x5rkl7k63i.cloudfront.net/upload/drug/brand/image/btMrpiACGw4508keMjgt8hchQbZUPG9kvkIfUDFIuUD6wyPDoU.jpg",
          "medium": "https://d1c0x5rkl7k63i.cloudfront.net/upload/drug/brand/image/btMrpiACGw4508keMjgt8hchQbZUPG9kvkIfUDFIuUD6wyPDoU.jpg",
          "small": "https://d1c0x5rkl7k63i.cloudfront.net/upload/drug/brand/image/kHAsYkdZTm8dWskM6hgRO2LyCTXNfvLLS3bV1SMcQrhfX6b9wz.jpg"
        },
        "brand_status": 1,
        "content": "Paroxetine HCl",
        "brand_info": [
          {
            "title": "Therapeutic class",
            "content": "Selective Serotonin Reuptake Inhibitors (SSRIs)"
          },
          {
            "title": "Regulatory Classification",
            "content": "Rx"
          },
          {
            "title": "Indications",
            "content": "Treatment for obsessive-compulsive disorder (OCD), panic disorder with or without agoraphobia, social anxiety disorder (SAD) or social phobia, post-traumatic stress disorder (PTSD), generalized anxiety disorder (GAD), all types of depression, including reactive and severe depression and depression accompanied by anxiety. Prevention of relapse and recurrence of further depressive episodes when used up to 1 yr."
          },
          {
            "title": "Dose",
            "content": "<b>Depression</b>: 20 mg daily, may be increased by 10 mg/day increments. Max 50 mg/day. <b>OCD and panic disorder</b>: 40 mg daily. Initially 10-20 mg daily, may be increased weekly by 10 mg increments. Max 60 mg/day. <b>SAD, GAD, PTSD</b>: 20 mg daily, may increase by 10 mg increments. Max 50 mg/day. <b>Elderly</b>: Adult starting dose, may be increased weekly by 10 mg/day increments up to 40 mg/day. <b>Renal/hepatic impairment</b>: Reduce dose."
          },
          {
            "title": "Contraindications",
            "content": "Hypersensitivity. Patients taking MAOIs."
          },
          {
            "title": "Precautions",
            "content": "Patients with MDD, mania and bipolar disorder, epilepsy, narrow-angle glaucoma, severe renal or hepatic impairment, recent history of MI or unstable heart disease. Patients taking MAOIs and ongoing ECT. May induce akathisia, serotonin syndrome/NMS, glaucoma, renal and hepatic impairment, hyponatremia, hemorrhage. May increase risk of suicidal thinking and behavior. Discontinue upon prolonged elevation on liver function test. Avoid abrupt discontinuation. Childn and adolescents &lt;18 yrs. Pregnancy and lactation. <b><a href=\"#\" id=\"has_preg_risk\">Pregnancy Risk</a></b>: Category C.",
            "pregnancy_risk": {
              "header": "Pregnancy Risk Category by the US FDA:",
              "categories": [
                {
                  "title": "A",
                  "content": "Adequate and compelling studies in humans do not show any evidence that the product poses a risk to the baby in the womb."
                },
                {
                  "title": "B",
                  "content": "Adequate and compelling studies in animals do not show a risk to the baby, but there are not enough compelling studies in humans. Otherwise, animal studies show a risk but studies on humans do not show any."
                },
                {
                  "title": "C",
                  "content": "Adequate and compelling studies in animals show a risk to the baby, but there are not enough compelling studies in humans. However, the product may be used in pregnant women in certain situations despite potential risks."
                },
                {
                  "title": "D",
                  "content": "Adequate and compelling studies in humans show a risk to the baby. However, the product may be used in pregnant women in certain situations despite the potential risks."
                },
                {
                  "title": "X",
                  "content": "Adequate and compelling studies in animals or humans show a risk to the baby in the womb. Also, use or experience in humans shows a risk. The danger of using the product in pregnant women clearly outweighs its benefits."
                }
              ]
            }
          },
          {
            "title": "Side Effects",
            "content": "Asthenia, constipation, decreased appetite, diarrhea, dizziness, dry mouth, insomnia, agitation, male sexual dysfunction, nausea, somnolence, sweating, tremor, elevation of hepatic enzymes, blurred vision."
          },
          {
            "title": "Drug Interactions",
            "content": "CYP2D6 inhibitors (thioridazine), fosamprenavir, ritonavir, procyclidine, anticonvulsants (carbamazepine, phenytoin, sodium valproate), pimozide."
          },
          {
            "title": "Packaging",
            "content": "Film-coated tablet 20 mg (P41.00)."
          }

  {
    "id": 1870,
    "detail": {
      "success": true,
      "data": {
        "id": 1870,
        "brand_name": "Xigduo XR",
        "brand_suffix": null,
        "brand_shot": {
          "original": null,
          "medium": null,
          "small": null
        },
        "brand_status": 1,
        "content": "Dapagliflozin propanediol monohydrate, metformin HCl",
        "brand_info": [
          {
            "title": "Therapeutic class",
            "content": "Sodium-Glucose Cotransporter 2 (SGLT2) Inhibitors"
          },
          {
            "title": "Regulatory Classification",
            "content": "Rx"
          },
          {
            "title": "Indications",
            "content": "Adjunct to diet and exercise. Prevention of new or worsening heart failure, nephropathy, or cardiovascular death."
          },
          {
            "title": "Dose",
            "content": "Taken once daily with evening meal. Do not crush, cut, or chew. Dapagliflozin 10 mg. Starting dose of metformin is 500 mg, can be titrated gradually to 2,000 mg to reduce the GI side effects. <b>Patients treated with metformin:</b> Same dose already being taken or at nearest therapeutically appropriate. <b>Renal impairment - eGFR 30-44 mL/min/1.73 m<sup>2</sup>:</b> Max 1,000 mg dapagliflozin, 10 mg/1,000 mg metformin."
          },
          {
            "title": "Contraindications",
            "content": "Hypersensitivity. Hepatic impairment. Severe renal impairment (eGFR < 30 mL/min/1.73 m<sup>2</sup>). Metabolic acidosis. Pregnancy (2<sup>nd</sup> and 3<sup>rd</sup> trimesters) and lactation."
          },
          {
            "title": "Precautions",
            "content": "Not for type 1 DM. Patients with risk for vol depletion, ketoacidosis, or hypoglycemia. Co-administration with drugs that can impair renal function (e.g., antihypertensives, diuretics, NSAIDs). Lower dose of insulin or insulin secretagogues to reduce risk of hypoglycemia. Initiation of metformin in patients with eGFR < 45 mL/min/1.73 m<sup>2</sup> is not recommended. Discontinue or temporary interrupt if ketoacidosis is suspected; lactic acidosis; conditions characterized by hypoxemia, dehydration, severe infections, and hypoperfusion; prior to or at the time of administration of IV iodinated contrast agents, and not reinstituted until 48 hrs after and only after renal function is found stable; Fournier’s gangrene; any surgical procedure (except minor procedures not associated with restricted intake of food and fluids) and do not restart until patient’s oral intake resumes and renal function is stable. Assess renal function prior to initiation of therapy and periodically thereafter; serum electrolytes and ketones, blood glucose, if indicated, blood pH, lactate, pyruvate, and metformin levels with type 2 DM previously well-controlled on treatment develop laboratory abnormalities or illness. Elderly. Childn and adolescents.",
            "pregnancy_risk": null
          },
          {
            "title": "Side Effects",
            "content": "GI symptoms, genital infections, UTI, pollakiuria, polyuria, back pain, vit B<sub>12</sub> decrease/deficiency, taste disturbance."
          },
          {
            "title": "Drug Interactions",
            "content": "Lithium, cationic drugs (e.g. amiloride, digoxin, morphine, procainamide, quinidine, quinine, ranitidine, triamterene, trimethoprim, vancomycin), glyburide, furosemide, nifedipine, thiazides and other diuretics, corticosteroids, phenothiazines, thyroid drugs, estrogens, oral contraceptives, phenytoin, nicotinic acid, sympathomimetics, CCBs, isoniazid. 1,5-anhydroglucitol assay."
          },
          {
            "title": "Packaging",
            "content": "Film-coated tablet 5 mg/500 mg, 5 mg/1,000 mg (P38.25), 10 mg/500 mg (P53.50), 10 mg/1,000 mg (P53.50)."
          }
        ],
        "company": {
          "id": 20,
          "name": "AstraZeneca Pharmaceuticals (Phils.), Inc."
        },
        "class": "human",
        "form_class": "3",
        "prescription_class": "Rx",

  {
    "id": 4430,
    "detail": {

      "data": {
        "id": 4430,

        "brand_suffix": null,
        "brand_shot": {
          "original": "https://d1c0x5rkl7k63i.cloudfront.net/upload/drug/brand/image/nQPMUkf3hZr899zDEua5M2WQS7OTAPqsX7E51LoY7l6RXxyaTc.jpg",
          "medium": "https://d1c0x5rkl7k63i.cloudfront.net/upload/drug/brand/image/KgdH2sM0voJhOepRx6TzSV6L42z7wa2956jgV8kYNhDcIAzOPb.jpg",
          "small": "https://d1c0x5rkl7k63i.cloudfront.net/upload/drug/brand/image/vXAiysOPFEeIV7Zlvi7SBTglfFZJnUUCh3kURz39uMuRyqwKdm.jpg"
        },
        "brand_status": 1,
        "content": "Sitagliptin phosphate",
        "brand_info": [
          {
            "title": "Therapeutic class",
            "content": "Dipeptidyl Peptidase-4 (DPP-4) Inhibitors"
          },
          {
            "title": "Regulatory Classification",
            "content": "Rx"
          },
          {
            "title": "Indications",
            "content": "To improve glycemic control in patients with type 2 diabetes mellitus: (1) As monotherapy in patients inadequately controlled by diet and exercise alone or for whom metformin is inappropriate; (2) in combination with metformin, a sulfonylurea, or a peroxisome proliferator-activated receptor gamma (PPAR-gamma) agonist (i.e. thiazolidinedione), as dual therapy, when diet and exercise plus the individual agent alone do not provide adequate glycemic control; (3) with a sulfonylurea or a PPAR-gamma agonist and metformin, as triple therapy, when diet and exercise plus dual therapy with these agents do not provide adequate glycemic control; (4) with insulin as an adjunct to diet and exercise."
          },
          {
            "title": "Dose",
            "content": "100 mg once daily. In combination with metformin and/or PPAR-gamma agonist: Maintain dose and administer sitagliptin concomitantly. In combination with sulfonylurea or with insulin: Consider lower dose for sulfonylurea or insulin. <b>Renal impairment</b> - Moderate (eGFR &ge;30-&lt;45 mL/min/1.73 m<sup>2</sup>): 50 mg once daily; Severe (eGFR &lt;30 mL/min/1.73 m<sup>2</sup>) or with ESRD requiring hemodialysis or peritoneal dialysis: 25 mg once daily."
          },
          {
            "title": "Contraindications",
            "content": "Hypersensitivity. Patients with type 1 DM. Lactation."
          },
          {
            "title": "Precautions",
            "content": "Not used to treat diabetic ketoacidosis. Patients with severe hepatic impairment, history of pancreatitis, heart failure. Discontinue if pancreatitis, hypersensitivity reactions, bullous pemphigoid are suspected. May cause severe joint pain. Concomitant use with other anti-hyperglycemic agents. Pregnancy. <b><a href=\"#\" id=\"has_preg_risk\">Pregnancy Risk</a>:</b> Category B.",
            "pregnancy_risk": {
              "header": "Pregnancy Risk Category by the US FDA:",
              "categories": [
                {
                  "title": "A",
                  "content": "Adequate and compelling studies in humans do not show any evidence that the product poses a risk to the baby in the womb."
                },
                {
                  "title": "B",
                  "content": "Adequate and compelling studies in animals do not show a risk to the baby, but there are not enough compelling studies in humans. Otherwise, animal studies show a risk but studies on humans do not show any."
                },
                {
                  "title": "C",
                  "content": "Adequate and compelling studies in animals show a risk to the baby, but there are not enough compelling studies in humans. However, the product may be used in pregnant women in certain situations despite potential risks."
                },
                {
                  "title": "D",
                  "content": "Adequate and compelling studies in humans show a risk to the baby. However, the product may be used in pregnant women in certain situations despite the potential risks."
                },
                {
                  "title": "X",
                  "content": "Adequate and compelling studies in animals or humans show a risk to the baby in the womb. Also, use or experience in humans shows a risk. The danger of using the product in pregnant women clearly outweighs its benefits."
                }
              ]
            }
          },
          {
            "title": "Side Effects",
            "content": "Hypoglycemia, headache."
          },
          {
            "title": "Drug Interactions",
            "content": "CYP3A4 inhibitors, metformin, ciclosporin, digoxin."
          },
          {
            "title": "Packaging",
            "content": "Film-coated tablet 50 mg (P24.00), 100 mg (P25.00)."
          }
        ],
        "company": {

          "name": "The Cathay YSS Distributors Co., Inc."
        },

          "small": "https://d1c0x5rkl7k63i.cloudfront.net/upload/drug/brand/image/YvEhIX8MOywF0rEeQK3FoIM10HAQ3mfJpizqOtmoHif3OICgY9.jpg"
        },
        "brand_status": 1,
        "content": "Sitagliptin phosphate monohydrate, metformin HCl",
        "brand_info": [
          {
            "title": "Therapeutic class",
            "content": "Dipeptidyl Peptidase-4 (DPP-4) Inhibitors"
          },
          {
            "title": "Regulatory Classification",
            "content": "Rx"
          },
          {
            "title": "Indications",
            "content": "Adjunct to diet and exercise to improve glycemic control in patients inadequately controlled on their maximal tolerated dose of metformin alone or those already being treated with a combination of sitagliptin and metformin. In triple combination with a sulfonylurea or a PPAR-gamma agonist (i.e. a thiazolidinedione) as adjunct to diet and exercise in patients inadequately controlled on maximal tolerated dose of metformin and a sulfonylurea or a PPAR-gamma agonist. Add-on to insulin as adjunct to diet and exercise to improve glycemic control in patients when stable dose of insulin and metformin alone do not provide adequate glycemic control."
          },
          {
            "title": "Dose",
            "content": "Individualized dosage. Initially sitagliptin 50 mg twice daily plus dose of metformin already being taken. Max daily sitagliptin dose: 100 mg. Max daily metformin dose: 2,000 mg."
          },
          {
            "title": "Contraindications",
            "content": "Hypersensitivity. Severe renal impairment (eGFR below 30 mL/min/1.73 m<sup>2</sup>). Metabolic acidosis, including ketoacidosis. Pregnancy and lactation."
          },
          {
            "title": "Precautions",
            "content": "Acute renal failure. Lactic acidosis, pancreatitis, heart failure, vitamin B deficiency, bullous pemphigoid, and severe and disabling arthralgia. Hypoglycemia with concomitant use of insulin or insulin secretagogues.",
            "pregnancy_risk": null
          },
          {
            "title": "Side Effects",
            "content": "Diarrhea, URTI, headache."
          },
          {
            "title": "Drug Interactions",
            "content": "Carbinic anhydrase inhibitors. Ranolazine, vandetanib, dolutegravir, and cimetidine. Alcohol."
          },
          {
            "title": "Packaging",
            "content": "Film-coated tablet 50 mg/500 mg, 50 mg/1 g."
          }
        ],

  {
    "id": 3973,
    "detail": {
      "success": true,
      "data": {
        "id": 3973,
        "brand_name": "Xipro",
        "brand_suffix": null,
        "brand_shot": {
          "original": "https://d1c0x5rkl7k63i.cloudfront.net/upload/drug/brand/image/gvXO7kznEzmqQFR5SgCmPyydwcCAmayRZDgnAjIZvNdmdeTc9P.jpg",
          "medium": "https://d1c0x5rkl7k63i.cloudfront.net/upload/drug/brand/image/Xi72ZVpbjdUiy1lvWy8USBptgdxL7b7txsw13aoruNdLVlzD8x.jpg",
          "small": "https://d1c0x5rkl7k63i.cloudfront.net/upload/drug/brand/image/9v32lNsbcIAxa9UoxEeubejvnKSy9LgNKL8OUJdag5ryOTQrDV.jpg"
        },
        "brand_status": 1,
        "content": "Ciprofloxacin",
        "brand_info": [
          {
            "title": "Therapeutic class",
            "content": "Quinolones"
          },
          {
            "title": "Regulatory Classification",
            "content": "Rx"
          },
          {
            "title": "Indications",
            "content": "Treatment of infections of the respiratory tract, middle ear, sinuses, eyes, kidneys, and urinary tract, genital organs, abdomen, skin and soft tissues, bones and joints; septicemia. Treatment of infections including animal bites, biliary tract infections, brucellosis, cat scratch disease, chancroid, gastroenteritis (including traveler’s diarrhea, cholera, salmonella enteritis, shigellosis), gonorrhea, immunocompromised patients, Legionnaire’s disease, meningitis prophylaxis, otitis, externa, peritonitis, Q fever, spotted fevers, typhoid and parathyroid fever, typhus."
          },
          {
            "title": "Dose",
            "content": "<b>Adults</b>: 250-750 mg twice daily depending on severity and nature of infection. <b>Prophylaxis of gonorrhea and meningococcal meningitis</b>: 500 mg single dose. <b>Duration of treatment</b>: 3 days; acute infections: 5-10 days. <b>Childn and adolescents (if considered essential)</b>: 5-15 mg/kg BW twice daily."
          },
          {
            "title": "Contraindications",
            "content": "Hypersensitivity. Childn and adolescents. Pregnancy and lactation."
          },
          {
            "title": "Precautions",
            "content": "Patients who suffered from CNS disorders e.g. lowered convulsion threshold, previous history of convulsion, reduced cerebral blood flow, stroke. Patients with aortic aneurysm and peripheral atherosclerotic vascular disease, Marfan syndrome, and Ehlers-Danlos syndrome. Elderly.",
            "pregnancy_risk": null
          },
          {
            "title": "Side Effects",
            "content": "GI disturbances e.g. nausea, vomiting, diarrhea, abdominal pain, dyspepsia. Pseudomembranous colitis. Headache, dizziness, restlessness, tremor, drowsiness, insomnia, visual disturbances. Peripheral neuropathy. Hypersensitivity reactions e.g. photosensitivity, vasculitis, SJS and toxic epidermal necrolysis. Increase in serum creatinine or BUN, acute renal failure secondary to intestinal nephritis, crystalluria. Elevated liver enzymes, hepatitis, eosinophilia, leucopenia, thrombocytopenia, myalgia and gynecomastia."
          },
          {
            "title": "Drug Interactions",
            "content": "Antacids, iron preparations, theophylline and warfarin. Preparations containing magnesium, aluminum or iron salts."
          },
          {
            "title": "Packaging",
            "content": "Tablet 500 mg (P36.04)."
          }
        ],

        },

          },
          {
            "title": "Indications",
            "content": "Symptomatic relief of nasal congestion due to common colds, upper respiratory allergies including hay fever and sinusitis."
          },
          {
            "title": "Dose",
            "content": "Adults and childn &ge;12 yrs: Apply 2-3 sprays in each nostril every 8-10 hrs."
          },
          {
            "title": "Contraindications",
            "content": "Hypersensitivity. Patients who recently underwent neurosurgery. Childn &lt;12 yrs."
          },
          {
            "title": "Precautions",
            "content": "For application in the nose only. Do not exceed recommended dose; not &gt;3 doses in 24 hrs. Do not use for &gt;7 consecutive days. Once opened, discard soln after 28 days. Patients with hyperthyroidism, glaucoma, DM, heart disease, HTN. Pregnancy and lactation.",
            "pregnancy_risk": null
          },
          {

            "content": "Transient local irritation in nose and throat, nausea, headache, dryness of nasal mucosa; systemic CV effects."
          },

            "title": "Drug Interactions",
            "content": "MAOIs, TCAs, tetracyclic antidepressants."
          },

  {
    "id": 1289,
    "detail": {
      "success": true,
      "data": {
        "id": 1289,
        "brand_name": "Xypen",
        "brand_suffix": null,
        "brand_shot": {
          "original": "https://d1c0x5rkl7k63i.cloudfront.net/upload/drug/brand/image/SBxUEuIQ509RkpKIwHxEHGCbtOIGbuEb7pAin6AFTHi6QRDYHI.jpg",
          "medium": "https://d1c0x5rkl7k63i.cloudfront.net/upload/drug/brand/image/SBxUEuIQ509RkpKIwHxEHGCbtOIGbuEb7pAin6AFTHi6QRDYHI.jpg",
          "small": "https://d1c0x5rkl7k63i.cloudfront.net/upload/drug/brand/image/JriPX7Q4ZGr2SZYxaVg1cnos2s2WjrjwSvRCVlCgF7pfGdVjAT.jpg"
        },
        "brand_status": 1,
        "content": "Ciprofloxacin HCl",
        "brand_info": [
          {
            "title": "Therapeutic class",
            "content": "Quinolones"
          },
          {
            "title": "Regulatory Classification",
            "content": "Rx"
          },
          {
            "title": "Indications",
            "content": "<b>Tab</b>: Treatment of infections e.g. gonorrhea, gynecological infections, surgical infection, acute uncomplicated urinary tract infection (UTI); prophylaxis of meningococcal meningitis, chronic bacterial prostatitis, inhalational anthrax (post-exposure in adult), lower respiratory tract infections (LRTI), acute sinusitis, infectious diarrhea, typhoid fever and infections in skin, bone and joint. <b>IV infusion</b>: Treatment of UTIs, RTIs, gastrointestinal infections, typhoid fever, bone and joint infections, general infection including blood poisoning."
          },
          {
            "title": "Dose",
            "content": "<b>Tab</b> - Adults: 250-750 mg twice daily. <b>Gonorrhea</b>: 250-500 mg as single dose. <b>Gynecological infection</b>: 500 mg twice daily. <b>Meningococcal meningitis prophylaxis</b>: 500 mg as single dose. <b>Prophylaxis of surgical infection</b>: 750 mg as a single dose 60-90 mins before surgery. <b>Acute uncomplicated UTI</b>: 250 mg twice daily for 3 days. <b>Chronic bacterial prostatitis</b>: 500 mg twice daily for 28 days. <b>Inhalational anthrax (post-exposure in adults)</b>: 500 mg twice daily. <b>LRTI, acute sinusitis, skin, bone and joint infections, infectious diarrhea, typhoid fever</b>: 500 mg twice daily. May be taken with or without food. <b>IV infusion</b>: 2 g daily with 12-hr interval and not < 30 mins. <b>Acute infection and infection caused by some bacteria</b>: May be increased to 0.8 g/day twice daily. <b>Course of treatment</b> - General UTI: 5-7 days; Complicated UTI: 7-14 days; Pneumonia, skin and soft tissue infection: 7-14 days; GI infections: 5-7 days; Bone and joint infection: 4-6 weeks; Typhoid fever: 10-14 days."
          },
          {
            "title": "Contraindications",
            "content": "Hypersensitivity to ciprofloxacin and other quinolones."
          },
          {
            "title": "Precautions",
            "content": "Convulsion, increased intracranial pressure and toxic psychosis. Avoid excessive sunlight. May impair ability to drive or operate machinery. Childn &lt;18 yrs. Elderly. <b><a href=\"#\" id=\"has_preg_risk\">Pregnancy Risk</a></b>: Category C.",
            "pregnancy_risk": {
              "header": "Pregnancy Risk Category by the US FDA:",
              "categories": [
                {
                  "title": "A",
                  "content": "Adequate and compelling studies in humans do not show any evidence that the product poses a risk to the baby in the womb."
                },
                {
                  "title": "B",
                  "content": "Adequate and compelling studies in animals do not show a risk to the baby, but there are not enough compelling studies in humans. Otherwise, animal studies show a risk but studies on humans do not show any."
                },
                {
                  "title": "C",
                  "content": "Adequate and compelling studies in animals show a risk to the baby, but there are not enough compelling studies in humans. However, the product may be used in pregnant women in certain situations despite potential risks."
                },
                {
                  "title": "D",
                  "content": "Adequate and compelling studies in humans show a risk to the baby. However, the product may be used in pregnant women in certain situations despite the potential risks."
                },
                {
                  "title": "X",

  {
    "id": 4982,
    "detail": {
      "success": true,
      "data": {
        "id": 4982,
        "brand_name": "YSS Benzylpenicillin Sodium",
        "brand_suffix": null,
        "brand_shot": {
          "original": null,
          "medium": null,
          "small": null
        },
        "brand_status": 1,
        "content": "Benzylpenicillin (as sodium)",
        "brand_info": [
          {
            "title": "Therapeutic class",
            "content": "Penicillins"
          },
          {
            "title": "Regulatory Classification",
            "content": "Rx"
          },
          {
            "title": "Indications",
            "content": "Treatment of staphylococci, streptococci, and pneumococci infections, clostridial infections (in conjunction with antitoxin and appropriate surgery); syphilis; anthrax; gonococci infections, meningococci infections (as an adjunct to sulfonamide therapy); erysipeloid; adjunct to treatment of Vincent’s infection (along with appropriate dental procedures); leptospirosis; relapsing fever; diphtheria (in conjunction with antitoxin); susceptible actinomycotic infections (in conjunction with other therapy); <i>Streptobacillus moniliformis</i> and <i>Spirillum minus</i> infections. Used prophylactically in major and minor surgery to prevent secondary infection, especially in tonsillectomies and tooth extractions in patients with rheumatic fever or rheumatic or CHD."
          },
          {
            "title": "Dose",
            "content": "<b>Serious acute infection with or without bacteremia:</b> 300,000-600,000 units every 4 hrs until patient’s temperature has been normal for 48 hours and no active infection remain. <b>Pneumococcic pneumonia:</b> 500,000 units injected IM every 12 hrs. <b>Gonorrhea:</b> 300,000-600,000 units inject IM every 4 hrs. <b>Syphilis </b> Primary or secondary syphilis and latent syphilis with negative spinal fluid – 4,800,000 units administered daily inj of 600,000 units each. Late syphilis – 6,000,000-9,000,000 units administered daily of 600,000 units. Early congenital syphilis (childn under 2 yrs) – 100,000 units/kg of BW given in divided doses at 2–3-day interval. Childn under 12, dosage should be adjusted to age and BW. <b>Meningitis:</b> 1,000,000 units IM every 2 hrs for a total of 12,000,000 units daily. <b>Bacterial endocarditis:</b> 600,000-2,000,000 units or more daily for 6-8 weeks, or longer every 2-3 hrs. <b>Prophylactic use in surgery:</b> 600,000 units or more daily in divided doses IM for 1 day before or several days after operation."
          },
          {
            "title": "Contraindications",
            "content": "Hypersensitivity."
          },
          {
            "title": "Precautions",
            "content": "Patients with bronchial asthma, other allergies, or those who have previously demonstrated a sensitivity to penicillin. Care is needed with very high doses of penicillin given, especially in renal impairment due to neurotoxicity. Monitor closely when administering large doses of potassium or sodium salts to patients with impaired renal function or heart failure. Monitor renal and hematological status during prolonged and high-dose therapy. Observe Jarisch-Herxheimer reaction when treating some patients with syphilis.",
            "pregnancy_risk": null
          },
          {
            "title": "Side Effects",
            "content": "Allergic reactions."
          },
          {
            "title": "Drug Interactions",
            "content": "Probenecid. Bacteriostatic antibacterial agents."
          },
          {
            "title": "Packaging",
            "content": "Sterile powder for injection 1,000,000 units (P287.28), 5,000,000 units (P831.04)."
          }
        ],
        "company": {
          "id": 214,
          "name": "The Cathay Drug Co., Inc."
        },

  {
    "id": 4083,
    "detail": {
      "success": true,
      "data": {
        "id": 4083,
        "brand_name": "Yulareb",
        "brand_suffix": null,
        "brand_shot": {
          "original": null,
          "medium": null,
          "small": null
        },
        "brand_status": 1,
        "content": "Abemaciclib",
        "brand_info": [
          {
            "title": "Therapeutic class",
            "content": "Protein Kinase Inhibitors"
          },
          {
            "title": "Regulatory Classification",
            "content": "Rx"
          },
          {
            "title": "Indications",
            "content": "In combination with endocrine therapy for the adjuvant treatment of adults with hormone receptor (HR)-positive, human epidermal growth factor receptor 2 (HER2)-negative, node positive early breast cancer at high risk of recurrence. In combination with an aromatase inhibitor as initial endocrine-based therapy for the treatment of postmenopausal women, and men, with HR-positive, HER2-negative advanced or metastatic breast cancer. In combination with fulvestrant for the treatment of adult patients with HR-positive, HER2-negative advanced or metastatic breast cancer with disease progression following endocrine therapy. As monotherapy for the treatment of adult patients with HR-positive, HER2-negative advanced or metastatic breast cancer with disease progression following endocrine therapy and prior chemotherapy in the metastatic setting."
          },
          {
            "title": "Dose",
            "content": "<b>Monotherapy:</b> 200 mg twice daily. <b>Combination with fulvestrant, tamoxifen, or an aromatase inhibitor:</b> 150 mg twice daily. <b>Treatment duration - Early breast cancer:</b> For 2 yrs or until disease recurrence or unacceptable toxicity occurs; <b>Advanced or metastatic breast cancer:</b> Continued until disease progression or unacceptable toxicity. <b>Combination with strong and moderate CYP3A4 inhibitors (other than ketoconazole):</b> Reduce dose to 50 mg (had dose reduction to 100 mg twice daily due to adverse reactions) or 100 mg twice daily (in patients with initial dose of 150 mg or 200 mg twice daily). <b>Severe hepatic impairment (Child Pugh-C):</b> Once daily. Taken at the same time every day, with or without food."
          },
          {
            "title": "Precautions",
            "content": "Discontinue if diarrhea requiring hospitalization occurs until resolve. Interstitial lung disease/pneumonitis. Interrupt dose in any grade of VTE in early and in metastatic breast cancer patients. Hepatotoxicity and hepatic transaminase elevation. Neutropenia. Monitor CBC and liver function tests prior to the start of therapy. May impair fertility in males. Use effective contraception in women during treatment and for at least 3 weeks after last dose. Childn. Pregnancy and lactation.",
            "pregnancy_risk": null
          },
          {
            "title": "Side Effects",
            "content": "Diarrhea, infections, fatigue, headache, nausea, abdominal pain, decreased appetite, vomiting, dehydration, anemia, neutropenia, leukopenia, lymphopenia, thrombocytopenia."
          },
          {
            "title": "Drug Interactions",
            "content": "Ketoconazole and other strong and moderate CYP3A inhibitors and inducers."
          },
          {
            "title": "Packaging",
            "content": "Film-coated tablet 50 mg, 100 mg, 150 mg, 200 mg."
          }
        ],
        "company": {
          "id": 221,
          "name": "Zuellig Pharma Corp."
        },

            "title": "Therapeutic class",
            "content": "Penicillins"
          },
          {
            "title": "Regulatory Classification",
            "content": "Rx"
          },
          {
            "title": "Indications",
            "content": "Syphilis, diphtheria, rheumatic fever, pharyngitis and spleen disorders."
          },
          {
            "title": "Dose",
            "content": "<b>URTI</b> - Adults: 1,200,000 U. Childn &ge;27.3 kg: 900,000 U; &le;27.3 kg and infants: 300,000-600,000 U. Given as single IM dose, 2 injections in 10 days. <b>Syphilis</b>: 2,400,000 U. <b>Tertiary and neurosyphilis</b>: 2,400,000 U and 600,000 U. As a single intramuscular dose/week for 3 weeks. <b>Congenital syphilis</b> - Childn 2-12 years: &frac14;-&frac13; adult dose; &le;2 yrs and infants: 50,000 U/kg BW. Given as single IM dose once/week for 2 weeks. <b>Prophylaxis for rheumatic fever, streptococcal pharyngitis</b>: 1,200,000 U as a single IM dose every 3 weeks."
          },
          {
            "title": "Contraindications",
            "content": "Hypersensitivity."
          },
          {
            "title": "Precautions",
            "content": "Hypersensitivity to cephalosporins and other beta-lactam antibiotics, poor renal function. Do not inject intravenously in patients with syphilis due to Jarisch-Herxheimer reactions. History of cardiac decompensation and severe heart disease. Do not inject into or near an artery or nerve. Lactation. <b><a href=\"#\" id=\"has_preg_risk\">Pregnancy Risk</a>:</b> Category B.",
            "pregnancy_risk": {
              "header": "Pregnancy Risk Category by the US FDA:",
              "categories": [
                {
                  "title": "A",
                  "content": "Adequate and compelling studies in humans do not show any evidence that the product poses a risk to the baby in the womb."
                },

                  "content": "Adequate and compelling studies in animals do not show a risk to the baby, but there are not enough compelling studies in humans. Otherwise, animal studies show a risk but studies on humans do not show any."
                },
                {
                  "title": "C",
                  "content": "Adequate and compelling studies in animals show a risk to the baby, but there are not enough compelling studies in humans. However, the product may be used in pregnant women in certain situations despite potential risks."
                },
                {
                  "title": "D",
                  "content": "Adequate and compelling studies in humans show a risk to the baby. However, the product may be used in pregnant women in certain situations despite the potential risks."

                  "title": "X",
                  "content": "Adequate and compelling studies in animals or humans show a risk to the baby in the womb. Also, use or experience in humans shows a risk. The danger of using the product in pregnant women clearly outweighs its benefits."
                }
              ]
            }
          },
          {
            "title": "Side Effects",
            "content": "Skin rash, anaphylaxis, angioedema, urticaria, hemolytic anemia, acute interstitial nephritis."
          },
