        "content": "Telmisartan, amlodipine besilate",

            "content": "Taken once daily. <b>Initial therapy:</b> 40 mg/5 mg or 80 mg/5 mg. Max dose 80 mg/10 mg once daily. <b>Hepatic impairment and Elderly &gt;75 yrs:</b> Initiate amlodipine therapy at 2.5 mg."
          },
          {
            "title": "Contraindications",
            "content": "Hypersensitivity."
          },
          {
            "title": "Precautions",
            "content": "Hypotension, hyperkalemia, dual blockade of the RAAS. Risk of Myocardial infarction or increased angina. Patients with impaired hepatic and renal function; heart failure. Pregnancy and lactation. <b><a href=\"#\" id=\"has_preg_risk\">Pregnancy Risk</a>:</b> Category D.",
            "pregnancy_risk": {

  {
    "id": 56,
    "detail": {
      "success": true,
      "data": {
        "id": 56,
        "brand_name": "Zanidip",
        "brand_suffix": null,
        "brand_shot": {
          "original": "https://d1c0x5rkl7k63i.cloudfront.net/upload/drug/brand/image/8Vwu6jM6coTLtmphbnT9DLp5me5buMnNppfcVchCCwC5eCgz6g.jpg",
          "medium": "https://d1c0x5rkl7k63i.cloudfront.net/upload/drug/brand/image/2JGWwn21HllebegxGgh6pJkVhmegemcmHvs5T2joR0rOObAuHf.jpg",
          "small": "https://d1c0x5rkl7k63i.cloudfront.net/upload/drug/brand/image/aiy082NQkqRTYE4OVC4c2BoxMigoYTR1r4AiXpK5iCldYklRvA.jpg"
        },
        "brand_status": 1,
        "content": "Lercanidipine HCl",
        "brand_info": [
          {
            "title": "Therapeutic class",
            "content": "Calcium Channel Blockers (Antihypertensives)"
          },
          {
            "title": "Regulatory Classification",
            "content": "Rx"
          },
          {
            "title": "Indications",
            "content": "Mild to moderate essential hypertension (HTN). May be administered together with other antihypertensives e.g. beta-blockers, diuretics, or ACE inhibitors."
          },
          {
            "title": "Dose",
            "content": "10 mg orally once daily before meals; may be increased to 20 mg depending on the individual patient’s response. Dose titration should be gradual because it may take about 2 weeks before the maximal antihypertensive effect is apparent. <b>Severe HTN:</b> Dose titration period should be reduced. Administer doses in the morning 15 mins, before breakfast."
          },
          {
            "title": "Contraindications",
            "content": "Hypersensitivity. Left ventricular outflow tract obstruction, untreated congestive cardiac failure, unstable angina pectoris or recent (within 1 month) MI. Severe hepatic and renal (GFR < 30 mL/min) impairment, including patients undergoing dialysis. Co-administration with strong CYP3A4 inhibitors, ciclosporin, grapefruit or grapefruit juice."
          },
          {
            "title": "Precautions",
            "content": "Sick sinus syndrome. IHD, left ventricular dysfunction, peritoneal dialysis. Mild to moderate renal or hepatic dysfunction. Concomitant use with CYP3A4 inducers, alcohol. Patients with rare hereditary problems of galactose intolerance, total lactase deficiency or glucose-galactose malabsorption. Childn. Pregnancy and lactation.",
            "pregnancy_risk": null
          },
          {
            "title": "Side Effects",
            "content": "Peripheral edema, headache, flushing, tachycardia, palpitations."
          },
          {
            "title": "Drug Interactions",
            "content": "CYP3A4 inhibitors (e.g. ketoconazole, itraconazole, ritonavir, erythromycin, troleandomycin, clarithromycin), ciclosporin, grapefruit or grapefruit juice, anticonvulsants (e.g. phenytoin, phenobarbital, carbamazepine), alcohol, terfenadine, astemizole. Class III anti-arrhythmic drugs (e.g. amiodarone, quinidine, sotalol), midazolam, digoxin."
          },
          {
            "title": "Packaging",
            "content": "Tablet 10 mg (P30.75), 20 mg (P41.75)."
          }
        ],
        "company": {
          "id": 10,
          "name": "Abbott Laboratories"
        },
        "class": "human",
        "form_class": "3",
        "prescription_class": "Rx",
        "description": null,

  {
    "id": 4808,
    "detail": {
      "success": true,
      "data": {
        "id": 4808,
        "brand_name": "Zanika",
        "brand_suffix": null,
        "brand_shot": {
          "original": "https://d1c0x5rkl7k63i.cloudfront.net/upload/drug/brand/image/LthWQlcwVrdXheTe8k1DaYufoFYxuSaFgBbqBUVEyhzLjRDkNu.jpg",
          "medium": "https://d1c0x5rkl7k63i.cloudfront.net/upload/drug/brand/image/ho2osTngIcGTT3uiZg4UAJaKrkD10ha79oUDwN1Y35AWj7HHl4.jpg",
          "small": "https://d1c0x5rkl7k63i.cloudfront.net/upload/drug/brand/image/3XAZ9Z8Tr489kAmsjmsVAMbDXWSqcv9AYrCEdfpFl5jE1CRN45.jpg"
        },
        "brand_status": 1,
        "content": "Hydroxychloroquine sulfate",
        "brand_info": [
          {
            "title": "Therapeutic class",
            "content": "Antimalarials"
          },
          {
            "title": "Regulatory Classification",
            "content": "Rx"
          },
          {
            "title": "Indications",
            "content": "Treatment of acute attacks of malaria due to <i>Plasmodium vivax, P. malariae, P. ovale</i> & susceptible strains of <i>P. falciparum</i>. Management of discoid and systemic lupus erythematosus, and rheumatoid arthritis (RA). <b>Adults:</b> Treatment of dermatological conditions caused or aggravated by sunlight. <b>Childn:</b> Treatment of juvenile idiopathic arthritis, in combination with other therapies."
          },
          {
            "title": "Dose",
            "content": "<b>Adults and elderly:</b> 200-400 mg daily. Max dose: 6.5 mg/kg/day calculated from ideal BW. <b>Patients to receive 400 mg daily:</b> Initially 400 mg daily in divided doses. Reduce to 200 mg when no further improvement is evident and increase to 400 mg if the response lessens. <b>Childn &ge;31 kg:</b> Max dose 6.5 mg/kg/day based on ideal BW. Discontinue treatment for rheumatic disease if there is no improvement in 6 months. Treatment for light-sensitive diseases should be given during periods of maximum exposure to light. Taken with food."
          },
          {
            "title": "Contraindications",
            "content": "Hypersensitivity. Galactose intolerance, Lapp lactase deficiency or glucose-galactose malabsorption. Pregnancy."
          },
          {
            "title": "Precautions",
            "content": "Patients should have an ophthalmological exam before initiating the treatment and yearly thereafter. Increased risk of retinopathy if max recommended dose exceeds. Discontinue immediately if patient develops a pigmentary abnormality, visual field defect, or any other explainable by difficulty in accommodation or presence corneal opacities. Monitor for signs and symptoms of cardiomyopathy. Patients with hepatic or renal disease, severe GI, neurological, or blood disorder. Patients with sensitivity to quinine, with G6PD deficiency, porphyria cutanae tarda, psoriasis. Periodic examination of skeletal muscle function reflexes with long term use. Risk of hypoglycemia. Extrapyramidal disorders. Childn. Lactation.",
            "pregnancy_risk": null
          },
          {
            "title": "Side Effects",
            "content": "Anorexia, affect ability, blurring of vision, diarrhea, vomiting, skin rash, pruritus."
          },
          {
            "title": "Drug Interactions",
            "content": "Digoxin, insulin or antidiabetic drugs, drugs that prolong QT interval, antimalarials (e.g., mefloquine), antiepileptics, methotrexate, ciclosporin, praziquantel, antacids, kaolin, cimetidine, ampicillin."
          },
          {
            "title": "Packaging",
            "content": "Film-coated tablet 200 mg (P55.00)."
          }
        ],
        "company": {
          "id": 790,
          "name": "Corbridge Group Phils., Inc."
        },
        "class": "human",

          "small": "https://d1c0x5rkl7k63i.cloudfront.net/upload/drug/brand/image/5qnzEPd4p7rQi3l9YUEzWsQmNFIllvYZbMXQ6vlU67eWftSkT4.jpg"
        },
        "brand_status": 1,
        "content": "Pyrazinamide",
        "brand_info": [
          {
            "title": "Therapeutic class",
            "content": "Tuberculosis & Leprosy"
          },
          {
            "title": "Regulatory Classification",
            "content": "Rx"
          },
          {
            "title": "Indications",
            "content": "All forms of pulmonary and extrapulmonary tuberculosis (TB) due to strains of <i>Mycobacterium tuberculosis</i> sensitive to pyrazinamide; as part of multidrug regimens to the treatment of TB, primarily in the 8-week phase of short-course treatment."
          },
          {
            "title": "Dose",
            "content": "Adults and childn: 35 mg/kg daily with max 3 g/day or 50 mg/kg 3x a week, or 75 mg/kg 2x a week. Partial intermittent therapy: Administer daily for the 1<sup>st</sup> 2 months with isoniazid and rifampicin, then continue isoniazid and rifampicin for the next 4 months."
          },
          {
            "title": "Contraindications",
            "content": "Hypersensitivity. Liver damage."
          },
          {
            "title": "Precautions",
            "content": "Renal impairment, gout, arthralgia, DM. Pregnancy and lactation. <b><a href=\"#\" id=\"has_preg_risk\">Pregnancy Risk</a></b>: Category C.",
            "pregnancy_risk": {
              "header": "Pregnancy Risk Category by the US FDA:",
              "categories": [
                {
                  "title": "A",
                  "content": "Adequate and compelling studies in humans do not show any evidence that the product poses a risk to the baby in the womb."
                },
                {
                  "title": "B",
                  "content": "Adequate and compelling studies in animals do not show a risk to the baby, but there are not enough compelling studies in humans. Otherwise, animal studies show a risk but studies on humans do not show any."
                },
                {
                  "title": "C",
                  "content": "Adequate and compelling studies in animals show a risk to the baby, but there are not enough compelling studies in humans. However, the product may be used in pregnant women in certain situations despite potential risks."
                },
                {
                  "title": "D",
                  "content": "Adequate and compelling studies in humans show a risk to the baby. However, the product may be used in pregnant women in certain situations despite the potential risks."
                },
                {
                  "title": "X",
                  "content": "Adequate and compelling studies in animals or humans show a risk to the baby in the womb. Also, use or experience in humans shows a risk. The danger of using the product in pregnant women clearly outweighs its benefits."
                }
              ]
            }
          },
          {
            "title": "Side Effects",
            "content": "Liver toxicity, anorexia, nausea, vomiting, arthralgia, malaise, fever, sideroblastic anemia and dysuria."
          },
          {
            "title": "Packaging",
            "content": "Suspension 250 mg/5 mL x 120 mL (P115.45)."
          }
        ],
        "company": {
          "id": 93,
          "name": "Natrapharm, Inc."
        },
        "class": "human",
        "form_class": "3",
        "prescription_class": "Rx",
        "description": null,

        "instrument": null,
        "sensitivity": null,

  {
    "id": 4510,
    "detail": {
      "success": true,
      "data": {
        "id": 4510,
        "brand_name": "Zeftrax S",
        "brand_suffix": null,
        "brand_shot": {
          "original": "https://d1c0x5rkl7k63i.cloudfront.net/upload/drug/brand/image/T05oDepRwSgi6yyakIJY7usH2tAWaup1Kv6XT4YihztKnqh6A1.jpg",
          "medium": "https://d1c0x5rkl7k63i.cloudfront.net/upload/drug/brand/image/XZjdukZNNYGByndOp1pFpoYqPJkgLKxGqWU3rXH6KpCwsmP3MA.jpg",
          "small": "https://d1c0x5rkl7k63i.cloudfront.net/upload/drug/brand/image/hKptM732LW8ge74y5P1qA1b0oFZRfyXnNyZwuIMeMLJtpxRclt.jpg"
        },
        "brand_status": 1,
        "content": "Ceftriaxone sodium, sulbactam sodium",
        "brand_info": [
          {
            "title": "Therapeutic class",
            "content": "Cephalosporins + Beta-Lactamase Inhibitors"
          },
          {
            "title": "Regulatory Classification",
            "content": "Rx"
          },
          {
            "title": "Indications",
            "content": "Treatment of the following infections caused by susceptible organisms: lower respiratory tract infections, acute bacterial otitis media, skin and skin structure infections, complicated and uncomplicated urinary tract infections, pelvic inflammatory disease, bacterial septicemia, bone and join infections, intra-abdominal infections, meningitis, sexually transmitted disease; and as surgical prophylaxis."
          },
          {
            "title": "Dose",
            "content": "<b>Adults</b>: Ceftriaxone 1-2 g/day IM/IV given once or in 2 equally divided doses; max 4 g/day. <b>Renal impairment (CrCl &lt;15 mL/min)</b>: Max 500 mg sulbactam every 12 hrs (1 g/day). <b>Childn</b>: Ceftriaxone 100 mg/kg IM/IV once daily or every 12 hrs; max 4 g/day. <b>Skin and skin structure infections</b>: Ceftriaxone 50-70 mg/kg/day IM/IV given once or in 2 equally divided doses; max 1 g/day. <b>Acute bacterial otitis media</b>: Ceftriaxone 50 mg/kg IM given as single dose; max 1 g. <b>Serious miscellaneous infections other than meningitis</b>: Ceftriaxone 50-75 mg/kg IM/IV every 12 hrs; max 2 g/day. <b>Duration of treatment</b>: 7-14 days; longer duration in complicated infections; 10 days for infections caused by <i>Streptococcus pyogenes</i>."
          },
          {
            "title": "Contraindications",
            "content": "Hypersensitivity."
          },
          {
            "title": "Precautions",
            "content": "Patients with known allergy to penicillin or other cephalosporins. Renal impairment. Patients with impaired vit K synthesis or low vit K stores (e.g. chronic hepatic disease and malnutrition) may require monitoring of PT during treatment. Vit K supplementation (10 mg/week) may be necessary if PT is prolonged before or during therapy.  Patients with history of GI disorders, especially colitis.",
            "pregnancy_risk": null
          },
          {
            "title": "Side Effects",
            "content": "Local reactions. <b>Ceftriaxone</b>: Phlebitis, rash, anemia, neutropenia, lymphopenia, thrombocytopenia, prolongation of PT, diarrhea, nausea, vomiting, dysgeusia, pseudomembranous colitis symptoms, headache, dizziness, Moniliasis, vaginitis, diaphoresis, flushing, presence of urine casts. Elevations of SGOT or SGPT, alkaline phosphatase, bilirubin, BUN, creatinine."
          },
          {
            "title": "Drug Interactions",
            "content": "Diuretics, aminoglycosides, chloramphenicol, alcohol, oral contraceptives, probenecid, Coombs test."
          },
          {
            "title": "Packaging",
            "content": "Power for injection (IM/IV) 1 g/500 mg."
          }
        ],
        "company": {
          "id": 1858,
          "name": "Labmate Pharma Inc."
        },
        "class": "human",
        "form_class": "3",
        "prescription_class": "Rx",
        "description": null,
        "directions": null,
        "instrument": null,
        "sensitivity": null,
        "uses": null,
        "withdrawal": null,
        "dangerous": 0,
        "storage": null,
        "paid": 0,
        "keywords": null,
        "generic_content": "Ceftriaxone + Sulbactam",
        "generic_content_ids": [
          2372
        ],

        "therapeutic_class": "Cephalosporins + Beta-Lactamase Inhibitors",
        "trusted_med_id": null,

        "show_brand_info": true

  {
    "id": 1064,
    "detail": {
      "success": true,
      "data": {
        "id": 1064,
        "brand_name": "Zefxon",
        "brand_suffix": null,
        "brand_shot": {
          "original": null,
          "medium": null,
          "small": null
        },
        "brand_status": 1,
        "content": "Omeprazole",
        "brand_info": [
          {
            "title": "Therapeutic class",
            "content": "Proton-Pump Inhibitors"
          },
          {
            "title": "Regulatory Classification",
            "content": "Rx"
          },
          {
            "title": "Indications",
            "content": "Alternative to oral medication in treatment of duodenal or gastric ulcer, Zollinger-Ellison syndrome, reflux esophagitis. Prophylaxis of acid aspiration."
          },
          {
            "title": "Dose",
            "content": "IV or IV infusion. Once daily, where oral medication is inappropriate. <b>Zollinger-Ellison syndrome</b>: 60 mg once daily. Higher daily doses maybe required and the dose should be divided and given twice daily. <b>Aspiration prophylaxis</b>: Administer 1 hr before operation."
          },
          {
            "title": "Contraindications",
            "content": "Hypersensitivity. Childn."
          },
          {
            "title": "Precautions",
            "content": "The possibility of malignancy should be excluded. Hepatic impairment. Pregnancy and lactation. <b><a href=\"#\" id=\"has_preg_risk\">Pregnancy Risk</a>:</b> Category C.",
            "pregnancy_risk": {
              "header": "Pregnancy Risk Category by the US FDA:",
              "categories": [
                {
                  "title": "A",
                  "content": "Adequate and compelling studies in humans do not show any evidence that the product poses a risk to the baby in the womb."
                },
                {
                  "title": "B",
                  "content": "Adequate and compelling studies in animals do not show a risk to the baby, but there are not enough compelling studies in humans. Otherwise, animal studies show a risk but studies on humans do not show any."
                },
                {
                  "title": "C",
                  "content": "Adequate and compelling studies in animals show a risk to the baby, but there are not enough compelling studies in humans. However, the product may be used in pregnant women in certain situations despite potential risks."
                },
                {
                  "title": "D",
                  "content": "Adequate and compelling studies in humans show a risk to the baby. However, the product may be used in pregnant women in certain situations despite the potential risks."
                },
                {
                  "title": "X",
                  "content": "Adequate and compelling studies in animals or humans show a risk to the baby in the womb. Also, use or experience in humans shows a risk. The danger of using the product in pregnant women clearly outweighs its benefits."
                }
              ]
            }
          },
          {
            "title": "Side Effects",
            "content": "GI infections, headache, dizziness, paresthesia, light headedness, feeling faint, somnolence, insomnia, vertigo, reversible mental confusion, agitation, aggression, depression, hallucinations, gynecomastia, diarrhea, constipation, abdominal pain, nausea, vomiting, flatulence, dry mouth, stomatitis, GI candidiasis, leukopenia, thrombocytopenia, agranulocytosis, pancytopenia, increased liver enzymes, encephalopathy, hepatitis, hepatic failure, arthralgia, myalgia, muscular weakness, impotence, rash or pruritus, urticaria, photosensitivity, bullous eruption, erythema multiforme, malaise, and hypersensitivity reactions."
          },
          {
            "title": "Drug Interactions",
            "content": "Diazepam, warfarin, phenytoin, ketoconazole, itraconazole, clarithromycin, ciclosporin, and disulfiram."
          },
          {
            "title": "Packaging",
            "content": "Lyophilized powder for injection (IV) 40 mg (P615.72)."
          }
        ],

  {
    "id": 4439,
    "detail": {
      "success": true,
      "data": {
        "id": 4439,
        "brand_name": "Zelera",
        "brand_suffix": null,
        "brand_shot": {
          "original": "https://d1c0x5rkl7k63i.cloudfront.net/upload/drug/brand/image/nRbKYWAuW4ooni0JIyj7IgCblKAxR7ibWzTOSgSEPzFIFk58VE.jpg",
          "medium": "https://d1c0x5rkl7k63i.cloudfront.net/upload/drug/brand/image/x5OO6zUg0YRX20i531cL7MCFPta9STYqe7LvuhZXVhYieb6tV6.jpg",
          "small": "https://d1c0x5rkl7k63i.cloudfront.net/upload/drug/brand/image/Qnwk0Ez9QZsxPvFQS8CT6dKBDftXVHVrrmDvJButuyIISnsAng.jpg"
        },
        "brand_status": 1,
        "content": "Hydrocortisone",
        "brand_info": [
          {
            "title": "Therapeutic class",
            "content": "Corticosteroids"
          },
          {
            "title": "Regulatory Classification",
            "content": "Rx"
          },
          {
            "title": "Indications",
            "content": "Treatment of primary or secondary adrenocortical insufficiency; systemic lupus erythematosus (SLE); severe erythema multiforme (Stevens-Johnson syndrome); bronchial asthma, anaphylactic reactions; ulcerative colitis, Crohn’s disease; gastric content aspiration; shock secondary to adrenocortical insufficiency or shock unresponsive to conventional therapy when adrenocortical insufficiency may be present."
          },
          {
            "title": "Dose",
            "content": "100-500 mg IV for 1-10 mins, repeated at intervals of 2, 4, or 6 hrs depending on the patient’s response and clinical condition. May be administered via IV injection (preferred), IV infusion, or IM injection."
          },
          {
            "title": "Contraindications",
            "content": "Hypersensitivity. Concomitant use with live or live, attenuated vaccines."
          },
          {
            "title": "Precautions",
            "content": "Avoid abrupt withdrawal. Not a replacement for conventional therapy. Not indicated for traumatic brain injury or stroke; cerebral edema associated with acute head injury or CVA. Should not be used for >72 hrs; it may be preferable to replace with methylprednisolone sodium succinate. Patients with hepatic impairment; pheochromocytoma; family or previous history or existing severe affective disorders (e.g. depressive or manic-depressive illness, steroid psychosis); osteoporosis, HTN, CHF, history or existing severe affective disorders (especially previous steroid psychosis), DM, history of TB, glaucoma, previous corticosteroid-induced myopathy, liver failure or cirrhosis, renal insufficiency, epilepsy, peptic ulceration, fresh intestinal anastomoses, predisposition to thrombophlebitis, abscess or other pyogenic infections, ulcerative colitis, diverticulitis, myasthenia gravis, ocular herpes simplex, corneal perforation, hypothyroidism, recent MI. Kaposi’s sarcoma; pheochromocytoma crisis; elevated BP, salt and water retention, increased potassium excretion; psychiatric adverse reactions. Growth retardation. Hypokalemia, susceptibility to infection or skin thinning. Observe patients subjected to severe stress closely for signs and symptoms of adrenocortical insufficiency. Elderly. May impair male fertility. Pregnancy and lactation.",
            "pregnancy_risk": null
          },
          {
            "title": "Drug Interactions",
            "content": "Ciclosporin, hepatic enzyme inducers (e.g. rifampicin, rifabutin, carbamazepine, phenobarbitone, phenytoin, primidone, aminoglutethimide), CYP3A4 inhibitors (e.g. cimetidine, erythromycin, ketoconazole, itraconazole, diltiazem, mibefradil), anticholinesterases, hypoglycemic agents (including insulin), antihypertensives and diuretics, carbenoxolone, coumarin anticoagulants, salicylates and NSAIDs, neuromuscular blockers (e.g. pancuronium)."
          },
          {
            "title": "Packaging",
            "content": "Powder for injection (IM/IV) 100 mg (P172.01)."
          }
        ],
        "company": {
          "id": 217,
          "name": "United American Pharmaceuticals, Inc."
        },

        "id": 3486,
        "brand_name": "Zeltine-MR",
        "brand_suffix": null,
        "brand_shot": {
          "original": "https://d1c0x5rkl7k63i.cloudfront.net/upload/drug/brand/image/K7T7s0PboTEUD48wxxF6RN5L7UPs3pwFHXAIiviG6GKe2TxqgO.jpg",
          "medium": "https://d1c0x5rkl7k63i.cloudfront.net/upload/drug/brand/image/jQo3h80TOuc1hp8xsEZa4dDiBhu5Rc70Lr5L7LdsrdPhrhkmCD.jpg",
          "small": "https://d1c0x5rkl7k63i.cloudfront.net/upload/drug/brand/image/wKdVOUQminv4S87JVz4MiS6iTEnR5UEQPJKcCDzNN0DoleCQyI.jpg"
        },
        "brand_status": 1,
        "content": "Gliclazide",
        "brand_info": [
          {
            "title": "Therapeutic class",
            "content": "Second Generation Sulfonylureas"
          },
          {
            "title": "Regulatory Classification",
            "content": "Rx"
          },
          {
            "title": "Indications",
            "content": "Type 2 diabetes mellitus in adults, in association with dietary measures with exercise."
          },
          {
            "title": "Dose",
            "content": "Initially 30 mg daily. May increase to 60, 90, or 120 mg in successive steps if blood glucose is not adequately controlled. Interval between each dose increment should be at least 1 month except in patients whose blood glucose has not reduced after 2 weeks of treatment. In such case, dose may be increased at the end of the 2<sup>nd</sup> week of treatment. Max 120 mg daily. 1 tab gliclazide 80 mg is comparable to 30 mg MR tab."
          },
          {
            "title": "Contraindications",
            "content": "Hypersensitivity. Type 1 diabetes, diabetic pre-coma and coma, diabetic keto-acidosis. Severe renal or hepatic insufficiency. Treatment with miconazole. Lactation."
          },
          {
            "title": "Precautions",
            "content": "Hypoglycemia, hepatic insufficiency or severe renal failure, poor blood glucose control, disturbance in blood glucose. Patients with G6PD deficiency; galactose intolerance, the Lapp lactase deficiency or glucose-galactose malabsorption. Discontinue treatment if cholestatic jaundice develops. May impair the ability to drive and operate machines. Childn and adolescents. Pregnancy.",
            "pregnancy_risk": null
          },
          {
            "title": "Side Effects",
            "content": "Hypoglycemia. Sweating, clammy skin, anxiety, tachycardia, HTN, palpitations, angina pectoris and cardiac arrhythmia. GI disturbances including abdominal pain, nausea, vomiting, dyspepsia, diarrhea and constipation; transient visual disturbances. Erythrocytopenia, agranulocytosis, hemolytic anemia, pancytopenia, allergic vasculitis, hyponatremia, elevated liver enzymes and impairment liver function."
          },
          {
            "title": "Drug Interactions",
            "content": "Miconazole; phenylbutazone; alcohol; other antidiabetic agents; beta-blockers; fluconazole, ACE inhibitors, H2-receptor antagonists, MAOIs, sulfonamides, clarithromycin and NSAIDs. Danazol; chlorpromazine; glucocorticoids and tetracosactrin; ritodrine, salbutamol, terbutaline (IV); St. John's wort. Fluoroquinolones; warfarin."
          },
          {
            "title": "Packaging",
            "content": "Modified-release tablet 30 mg (P6.75), 60 mg (P12.50)."
          }
        ],

        "content": "Cefixime trihydrate",
        "brand_info": [
          {
            "title": "Therapeutic class",
            "content": "Third Generation Cephalosporins"
          },
          {
            "title": "Regulatory Classification",
            "content": "Rx"
          },
          {
            "title": "Indications",
            "content": "Treatment of the following infections caused by susceptible organisms: respiratory tract infections, enteric fever, urinary tract infections, otitis media, gonorrhea; sequential treatment after initial IV chemotherapy after parenteral cephalosporin."
          },
          {
            "title": "Dose",
            "content": "<b>Adults and Childn >12 yrs</b>: 400 mg as single dose or in 2 divided doses. <b>Childn</b>: 8 mg/kg/day as single dose or in 2 divided doses. <b>Renal impairment</b>: CrCl 21-60 mL/min or on hemodialysis: 75% of standard dose at the standard dosing interval; CrCl &lt;20 mL/min or on CAPD: 50% of standard dose at the standard dosing interval. <b>Course of treatment</b>: 7-14 days."
          },
          {
            "title": "Contraindications",
            "content": "Hypersensitivity."
          },
          {
            "title": "Precautions",
            "content": "Patients who have history of hypersensitivity to penicillins and other cephalosporins. May cause antibiotic-associated diarrhea. Patients with markedly impaired renal function; at risk for GI disease particularly pseudomembranous colitis. Childn &lt;6 months. Pregnancy and lactation.",
            "pregnancy_risk": null
          },
          {
            "title": "Side Effects",
            "content": "GI disturbances (e.g. alteration of normal flora of the colon, diarrhea, stool changes), pseudomembranous colitis, headache, dizziness, thrombocytopenia, leucopenia, eosinophilia."
          },
          {
            "title": "Drug Interactions",
            "content": "May yield false-positive reaction for urinary ketones with nitroprusside test; urinary glucose with Benedict's soln, Fehling's soln, or copper sulfate test cap; direct Coombs test."
          },
          {
            "title": "Packaging",
            "content": "Capsule 200 mg."
          }

  {

    "detail": {
      "success": true,
      "data": {
        "id": 4964,
        "brand_name": "Zemidapa",
        "brand_suffix": null,
        "brand_shot": {
          "original": "https://d1c0x5rkl7k63i.cloudfront.net/upload/drug/brand/image/9JnCuM2xWci5QOzPTCEsldWvwJoACENrjwWrDOHndv6Z7SrYmw.jpg",
          "medium": "https://d1c0x5rkl7k63i.cloudfront.net/upload/drug/brand/image/u98YUKukkB7TikdMdcfJjcJDrmZpOq7PmjIuxN9mRupGlpGYkn.jpg",
          "small": "https://d1c0x5rkl7k63i.cloudfront.net/upload/drug/brand/image/tp4pxCD5sdevl60eYFMR68eqoH3SbWUED1ITJt45Evbsd095oA.jpg"
        },
        "brand_status": 1,
        "content": "Gemigliptin (as tartrate sesquihydrate), dapagliflozin",
        "brand_info": [
          {
            "title": "Therapeutic class",
            "content": "Dipeptidyl Peptidase-4 (DPP-4) Inhibitors"
          },
          {
            "title": "Regulatory Classification",
            "content": "Rx"
          },
          {
            "title": "Indications",
            "content": "Adjunct to diet and exercise to improve glycemic control in adults with type 2 diabetes mellitus (DM)."
          },
          {
            "title": "Dose",
            "content": "1 tab once daily with or without food."
          },
          {
            "title": "Contraindications",
            "content": "Hypersensitivity. Type 1 DM. Diabetic ketoacidosis (DKA). Rare hereditary galactose intolerance, total lactase deficiency, glucose-galactose malabsorption. Hemodialysis. Pregnancy and lactation."
          },
          {
            "title": "Precautions",
            "content": "Patients with severe hepatic impairment; NYHA class I or II; history of pancreatitis; taking antihypertensive therapy with history of hypotension; predisposition to DKA; history of genital mycotic infections. Consider dose reduction of concomitant sulfonylurea or insulin. May induce severe and disabling arthralgia; volume depletion, hypotension; positive test for glucose in urine; increased risk of genital mycotic infections; weight loss; lower limb amputations. Evaluate renal function prior to initiation of treatment. Not recommended to patients with eGFR < 45 mL/min/1.73m<sup>2</sup> or NYHA class III or IV. Discontinue if pancreatitis, bullous pemphigoid, severe hypersensitivity (e.g., SJS), DKA, or Fournier’s gangrene occur. Childn < 18 yrs. Elderly.",
            "pregnancy_risk": null
          },
          {
            "title": "Side Effects",
            "content": "Increased lipase, chronic gastritis, dizziness, UTI, cough, diabetic nephropathy, gastritis, gingivitis, large intestine polyp, urticaria, dyspepsia."
          },
          {
            "title": "Drug Interactions",
            "content": "<b>Gemigliptin:</b> Strong CYP3A4 inducers (e.g., rifampicin, dexamethasone, phenytoin, carbamazepine, rifabutin, phenobarbital). <b>Dapagliflozin:</b> Thiazide and loop diuretics, insulin and insulin secretagogue."
          },
          {
            "title": "Packaging",
            "content": "Film-coated tablet 50 mg/10 mg (P69.95)."
          }
        ],

        },
        "brand_status": 1,
        "content": "Gemigliptin tartrate sesquihydrate",
        "brand_info": [
          {
            "title": "Therapeutic class",
            "content": "Dipeptidyl Peptidase-4 (DPP-4) Inhibitors"
          },
          {
            "title": "Regulatory Classification",
            "content": "Rx"
          },
          {
            "title": "Indications",
            "content": "Treatment of type 2 diabetes mellitus (DM) in patients 18-75 yrs. As monotherapy in adjunct to diet and exercise to improve glycemic control in adults with type 2 DM. Dual therapy, in combination with metformin as initial therapy in treatment of patients with inadequate glycemic control by diet and exercise alone or with max tolerated dose of metformin alone. Triple therapy in combination with metformin and sulfonylurea in patients with inadequate glycemic control with max tolerated dose of metformin and sulfonylurea dual therapy."
          },
          {
            "title": "Dose",
            "content": "50 mg once daily."
          },
          {
            "title": "Contraindications",
            "content": "Hypersensitivity. Type 1 DM or diabetic ketoacidosis."
          },
          {
            "title": "Precautions",
            "content": "Hypersensitivity. NYHA class I cardiac status. Not recommended in patients with NYHA class II-IV cardiac status. History of pancreatitis. Patients with hypoglycemia, severe and disabling arthralgia. Childn &lt;18 yrs. Pregnancy and lactation.",
            "pregnancy_risk": null
          },
          {
            "title": "Side Effects",
            "content": "Dyspepsia, hypoglycemia."
          },
          {
            "title": "Drug Interactions",
            "content": "Metformin, pioglitazone, ketoconazole, rifampicin, rosuvastatin."
          },
          {
            "title": "Packaging",
            "content": "Film-coated tablet 50 mg (P45.15)."
          }
        ],

  {
    "id": 3883,
    "detail": {
      "success": true,
      "data": {
        "id": 3883,
        "brand_name": "Zemimet SR",
        "brand_suffix": null,
        "brand_shot": {
          "original": "https://d1c0x5rkl7k63i.cloudfront.net/upload/drug/brand/image/g9jxEbUjJodtZj2ElFvcbjraXlrPDaYB6eLyJ46YgcwJm7CM4p.jpg",
          "medium": "https://d1c0x5rkl7k63i.cloudfront.net/upload/drug/brand/image/LILxsqsA8Om4Abfl2fXNgNQ99KUJu7D40BfOJ193VSMX9kkyuq.jpg",
          "small": "https://d1c0x5rkl7k63i.cloudfront.net/upload/drug/brand/image/VRpLd3HK7SJhrIwFdkesY6Csrjhal5c9KdGaZxYZ49vDcBFyb9.jpg"
        },
        "brand_status": 1,
        "content": "Gemigliptin tartrate sesquihydrate, metformin HCl",
        "brand_info": [
          {
            "title": "Therapeutic class",
            "content": "Dipeptidyl Peptidase-4 (DPP-4) Inhibitors"
          },
          {
            "title": "Regulatory Classification",
            "content": "Rx"
          },
          {
            "title": "Indications",
            "content": "Adjunct to diet and exercise to improve glycemic control in adults with type 2 diabetes mellitus (DM). Initial therapy in patients with inadequate glycemic control by diet and exercise alone, or with max tolerated dose of metformin alone. In combination with sulfonylurea in patients inadequately controlled on max tolerated dose of metformin alone and a sulfonylurea. In combination with insulin in patients with inadequate glycemic control with insulin monotherapy or insulin and metformin dual therapy. In patients already receiving an identical combination of gemigliptin and metformin as separate tabs."
          },
          {
            "title": "Dose",
            "content": "Individualized dosage. 1 tab of 50 mg/500 mg or 50 mg/1g once daily with meals in the evening. <b>Patients who need 50 mg gemigliptin and not currently treated with metformin</b>: Initially 50 mg gemigliptin and 500 mg metformin extended-release once daily with gradual dose escalation. <b>Patients with inadequate glycemic control on metformin monotherapy, dual combination of metformin and sulfonylurea or metformin and insulin</b>: 50 mg gemigliptin and the dose of metformin already being taken. <b>Patients switching from co-administration of gemigliptin and metformin as separate tabs</b>: Initiate the dose already being taken. Max: 50 mg gemigliptin and 2,000 mg metformin. When in combination with sulfonylurea or insulin, use a lower dose of sulfonylurea or insulin."
          },
          {
            "title": "Contraindications",
            "content": "Hypersensitivity."
          },
          {
            "title": "Precautions",
            "content": "Renal impairment (serum creatinine &ge;15 mg/dL for men, &ge;14 mg/dL for women, or abnormal CrCl). CHF requiring medication. For any surgical procedure (except minor procedures not associated with restricted intake of food and fluids) or radiologic studies involving use of IV iodinated contrast materials, temporarily discontinue treatment at least 48 hrs prior to and 48 hrs after the procedure and reinstitute only after renal function has been re-evaluated and found to be normal. Patients with type 1 diabetes or metabolic acidosis including diabetic ketoacidosis with or without coma, or history of ketoacidosis. Suspend therapy in presence of severe infection or traumatism and not be restarted until the patient’s oral intake has resumed and renal function is normal. Pre-diabetic coma. Debilitated, malnourished, or starved patients, and those with adrenal or pituitary insufficiency. Hepatic impairment. Pulmonary infarction, severe respiratory insufficiency, and any conditions that induce hypoxemia; promptly discontinue if hypoxic states occur. Excessive alcohol, dehydration, GI symptoms e.g. diarrhea and vomiting. Childn. Elderly. Pregnancy and lactation. <b>Gemigliptin</b>: History of pancreatitis. Risk of hypoglycemia with sulfonylurea; severe and disabling arthralgia. Discontinue use if bullous pemphigoid is suspected. <b>Metformin</b>: Lactic acidosis; evaluation of serum electrolytes, ketones, blood glucose, blood pH, lactate, lactate/pyruvate ratio, and metformin may be useful. Hypoglycemia due to deficient caloric intake, strenuous exercise without caloric supplementation, or concomitant use with other glucose-lowering agents (e.g. sulfonylurea and insulin) or alcohol. Patients with inadequate vit B<sub>12</sub> or calcium intake or absorption. Concomitant use with cationic drugs.",
            "pregnancy_risk": null
          },
          {
            "title": "Side Effects",
            "content": "Dyspepsia, dizziness, dyslipidemia, headache, constipation. <b>Gemigliptin</b>: Arthralgia, nasopharyngitis, bacteriuria, URTI, increased blood amylase and lipase; pyrexia. <b>Metformin</b>: Diarrhea; nausea, vomiting."
          },
          {
            "title": "Drug Interactions",
            "content": "<b>Gemigliptin</b>: Metformin (2,000 mg). Pioglitazone. Strong CYP3A4 inducers e.g. rifampicin, dexamethasone, phenytoin, carbamazepine, rifabutin and phenobarbital. Ketoconazole. <b>Metformin</b>: Insulin, sulfonylamides, sulfonylureas, alpha-glucosidase inhibitors, anabolic steroids, guanethidine, salicylates (aspirin), beta-blockers (propranolol), MAOIs, ARBs. Epinephrine, sympathomimetics, corticosteroids, thyroid hormones, follicle hormone, estrogen, oral contraceptives, thiazides and other diuretics, pyrazinamide, isoniazid, nicotinic acid, phenothiazines, phenytoin, CCBs. Alcohol or medicinal products that contain alcohol. Iodinated contrast agents. Furosemide. Nifedipine. OCT1 inhibitors (verapamil). OCT1 inducers (rifampicin). OCT2 inhibitors (cimetidine, dolutegravir, lanolazine, trimethoprim, vandetanib, isavuconazole). OCT2 and OCT1 co-inhibitors (crizotinib, olaparib)."
          },
          {
            "title": "Packaging",
            "content": "Film-coated tablet 50 mg/500 mg (P49.95), 50 mg/1 g (P49.95)."
          }
        ],
        "company": {
          "id": 3911,
          "name": "GX International, Inc."
        },
        "class": "human",
        "form_class": "3",
        "prescription_class": "Rx",

    "id": 1052,
    "detail": {

      "data": {

        "brand_name": "Zenith",
        "brand_suffix": null,
        "brand_shot": {
          "original": "https://d1c0x5rkl7k63i.cloudfront.net/upload/drug/brand/image/vF6l8IkT2Ecymw22Lc3K0ol4EWdgvsc6Tyysa44EHBIhdng6no.jpg",
          "medium": "https://d1c0x5rkl7k63i.cloudfront.net/upload/drug/brand/image/FrN8JJ6M0aRqpJz6yUFypERWIgGQN6oA1Zi3igJinmaf7xYOSb.jpg",
          "small": "https://d1c0x5rkl7k63i.cloudfront.net/upload/drug/brand/image/NT5Ijj8ZeI8I50FL1QcARzYCe9WxBtQDywBDhsb5AFdHbs07k0.jpg"
        },
        "brand_status": 1,
        "content": "Azithromycin",
        "brand_info": [
          {
            "title": "Therapeutic class",
            "content": "Macrolides"
          },
          {
            "title": "Regulatory Classification",
            "content": "Rx"
          },
          {
            "title": "Indications",
            "content": "Treatment of respiratory tract infections, otitis media, skin and soft tissue infections, uncomplicated genital chlamydial infections, non-gonococcal urethritis, mild or moderate typhoid due to multiple antibacterial-resistant organisms. Prophylaxis of endocarditis in childn."
          },
          {
            "title": "Dose",
            "content": "<b>Adults:</b> 500 mg once daily for 3 days. Alternatively, initially 500 mg, may be followed by 250 mg daily for 4 days. <b>Uncomplicated genital infections due to <i>Chlamydia trachomatis</i>, chancroid:</b> 1 g as a single dose. <b>Granuloma inguinale:</b> Initially 1 g, followed by 500 mg daily. Or 1 g once weekly for at least 2 weeks until lesions have completely healed. <b>Prophylaxis of disseminated MAC infections:</b> 1.2 g once weekly. <b>Treatment or secondary prophylaxis:</b> 50 mg once daily, given with other antimycobacterial. <b>Mild or moderate typhoid caused by multidrug-resistant strains:</b> 500 mg once daily for 7 days."
          },
          {
            "title": "Contraindications",
            "content": "Hypersensitivity."
          },
          {
            "title": "Precautions",
            "content": "Not indicated for viral infections. Full course of therapy should be completed. Patients with hepatic impairment. Concomitant use with aluminum and magnesium-containing antacids. Prolonged cardiac repolarization and QT interval.",
            "pregnancy_risk": null
          },
          {
            "title": "Side Effects",
            "content": "Nausea, vomiting, diarrhea, abdominal pain."
          },
          {
            "title": "Drug Interactions",
            "content": "Aluminum and magnesium-containing antacids, theophylline, warfarin, didanosine. Digoxin, ergotamine or dihydroergotamine, triazolam, CYP450 metabolized drugs (e.g. carbamazepine, ciclosporin, hexobarbital, phenytoin)."
          },
          {
            "title": "Packaging",
            "content": "Film-coated tablet 500 mg (P144.75)."
          }
        ],

  {
    "id": 1186,
    "detail": {
      "success": true,
      "data": {
        "id": 1186,
        "brand_name": "Zenith Powder for Oral Suspension",
        "brand_suffix": null,
        "brand_shot": {
          "original": "https://d1c0x5rkl7k63i.cloudfront.net/upload/drug/brand/image/7czvKq8AttTrBjh40Ir8vvXGv5PYOH1W8Rif3uwFkwekPPuVAc.jpg",
          "medium": "https://d1c0x5rkl7k63i.cloudfront.net/upload/drug/brand/image/wGNoxQNQDveP8RIhqiOnnIxvPTMdpxpbDSPAlugFGcSd0MBKKh.jpg",
          "small": "https://d1c0x5rkl7k63i.cloudfront.net/upload/drug/brand/image/XexnDu0kMge8mZMUPcvsX5URjZjAiKkaQlqRaNKwjOD4WQTNTt.jpg"
        },
        "brand_status": 1,
        "content": "Azithromycin monohydrate",
        "brand_info": [
          {
            "title": "Therapeutic class",
            "content": "Macrolides"
          },
          {
            "title": "Regulatory Classification",
            "content": "Rx"
          },
          {
            "title": "Indications",
            "content": "Treatment of upper (e.g. sinusitis, pharyngitis, tonsillitis) and lower (e.g. bronchitis, pneumonia) respiratory tract infections, acute otitis media, skin and soft tissue infections, uncomplicated genital infections caused by non-multi-resistant strains of <i>Chlamydia trachomatis</i> or <i>Neisseria gonorrhea</i>."
          },
          {
            "title": "Dose",
            "content": "<b>Adults and adolescents >45 kg -</b> 3-day therapy: 500 mg (12.5 mL)/day; 5-day therapy: 500 mg (12.5 mL) for day 1, then 250 mg (6.25 mL) for days 2-5. <b>Uncomplicated <i>C. trachomatis</i> or <i>N. gonorrhoeae</i>-associated genital infections:</b> 1,000 mg (25 mL). <b>Pneumonia -</b> 3-day or 5-day therapy regimen. <b>Adolescents and childn &le;45 kg -</b> 3-day therapy: 10 mg/kg/day; 5-day therapy: 10 mg/kg for day 1, then 5mg/kg for days 2-5. <b>Acute otitis media:</b> 30 mg/kg as single dose, 3-day therapy, or 5-day therapy. <b>Streptococcal pharyngitis:</b> 10-20 mg/kg/day for 3 days. Max 500 mg  Measure dose using the included 10 mL dosing syringe. Shake vigorously before each use."
          },
          {
            "title": "Contraindications",
            "content": "Hypersensitivity to any macrolide or ketolide antibiotics."
          },
          {
            "title": "Precautions",
            "content": "Not indicated for serious infections. Not for long-term use. Cross-resistance with macrolides, erythromycin and methicillin-resistant G(+) strains. Patients with significant hepatic disease (e.g. pre-existing hepatic disease or taking other hepatotoxic drugs); severe renal impairment (e.g GFR < 10 mL/min); ongoing proarrhythmic conditions (e.g. congenital or documented QT prolongation, currently receiving treatment with other QT-prolonging agents, electrolyte disturbances, clinically relevant bradycardia, cardiac arrhythmia, or severe cardiac insufficiency) especially women and elderly; neurological and psychiatric disorders. Rare serious allergic (e.g. angioneurotic edema, anaphylaxis) and dermatologic reactions (e.g. AGEP, SJS, TEN), DRESS. Discontinue treatment if signs and symptoms of liver dysfunction occur. Pseudomembranous colitis, ergotism, superinfection, CDAD, prolonged cardiac repolarization and QT interval, myasthenia gravis. Childn. Elderly. Pregnancy and lactation. <b>Neonates:</b> IHPS.",
            "pregnancy_risk": null
          },
          {
            "title": "Side Effects",
            "content": "Diarrhea. Headache; vomiting, abdominal pain, nausea; decreased lymphocytes and blood bicarbonate; increased eosinophils, basophils, monocytes, and neutrophils. Flatulence, abdominal discomfort, loose stools."
          },
          {
            "title": "Drug Interactions",
            "content": "Antacids, digoxin, colchicine, ergot derivatives, zidovudine, atorvastatin, coumarin-type oral anticoagulants, ciclosporin, fluconazole, medicines that prolong QT interval."
          },
          {
            "title": "Packaging",
            "content": "Powder for suspension 200 mg/5 mL x 20 mL (P567.00)."
          }
        ],
        "company": {

        },

    "id": 4163,
    "detail": {
      "success": true,
      "data": {
        "id": 4163,
        "brand_name": "Zenon",
        "brand_suffix": null,
        "brand_shot": {
          "original": "https://d1c0x5rkl7k63i.cloudfront.net/upload/drug/brand/image/sYxXKTdA2LQYEUkYz9hxxBH2wfr9mTAfajLWxTfEKSEGQBu7av.jpg",
          "medium": "https://d1c0x5rkl7k63i.cloudfront.net/upload/drug/brand/image/HgstqyMNoEaqixGHaaKTYvnoSrAxMsROxc1cnB6aGGVK1lKGKR.jpg",
          "small": "https://d1c0x5rkl7k63i.cloudfront.net/upload/drug/brand/image/WXaS1fVj615caHhOsYxchILC0vlN95h7FOoAYtoxxz4hoEeBuh.jpg"
        },
        "brand_status": 1,
        "content": "Rosuvastatin, ezetimibe",
        "brand_info": [
          {
            "title": "Therapeutic class",
            "content": "Lipid Modifying Agent Combination"
          },
          {
            "title": "Regulatory Classification",
            "content": "Rx"
          },
          {
            "title": "Indications",
            "content": "Treatment of primary hypercholesterolemia (heterozygous familial and non-familial) or homozygous familial hypercholesterolemia in adult patients: (i) who are not appropriately controlled with statin alone, (ii) who are adequately controlled with rosuvastatin and ezetimibe given concurrently at the same dose level but as separate products. Substitution therapy in adult patients who are adequately controlled with rosuvastatin and ezetimibe given concurrently, as separate products, to reduce the risk of cardiovascular events in patients with coronary heart disease and a history of acute coronary syndrome."
          },
          {
            "title": "Dose",
            "content": "1 tab daily. Co-administration with bile acid sequestrants: Administer tab &ge;2 hrs before or &ge;4 hrs after bile acid sequestrants."
          },
          {
            "title": "Contraindications",
            "content": "Hypersensitivity. Severe renal impairment (CrCl &lt;30 mL/min). Patients with moderate (Child-Pugh score 7-9) or severe (Child-Pugh score &gt;9) liver dysfunctions. Patients with myopathy. Patients receiving concomitant ciclosporin. Active liver disease or unexplained persistent elevations in serum transaminases and any serum transaminase elevation exceeding 3x ULN. Women of childbearing potential not using appropriate contraceptive measures. Pregnancy and lactation."
          },
          {
            "title": "Precautions",
            "content": "Not suitable for initial therapy. Predisposing factors for myopathy/rhabdomyolysis. Moderate renal impairment. Moderate or severe hepatic impairment. Secondary hypercholesterolemia caused by hypothyroidism or nephrotic syndrome should be treated prior to initiating therapy with rosuvastatin. Concomitant use of protease inhibitors. Alcohol consumption. History of liver disease. Do not use within 7 days after last systemic fusidic acid treatment. Patients with galactose intolerance, total lactase deficiency, or glucose-galactose malabsorption. May impair ability to drive and operate machinery. Asian. Childn &lt;18 yrs. Elderly &gt;70 yrs.",
            "pregnancy_risk": null
          },
          {
            "title": "Side Effects",
            "content": "DM. Headache, dizziness. Constipation, nausea, abdominal pain, diarrhea, flatulence. Myalgia, elevation of ALT and/or AST, asthenia, fatigue."
          },
          {
            "title": "Drug Interactions",
            "content": "Ciclosporin, warfarin. Fibrates and other lipid-lowering products, antacids, cholestyramine, anticoagulants, vit K antagonist. Gemfibrozil, protease inhibitors, transporter protein inhibitors, fusidic acid, CYP450 enzymes, clopidogrel, ticagrelor, erythromycin, oral contraceptive/hormone replacement therapy (HRT)."
          },
          {
            "title": "Packaging",
            "content": "Film-coated tablet 10 mg/10 mg (P51.00), 20 mg/10 mg (P55.50)."
          }
        ],

        "brand_shot": {
          "original": "https://d1c0x5rkl7k63i.cloudfront.net/upload/drug/brand/image/ZsFLd8KylabWf9NtKRwYjNhSnhERwb5J1kqj5C8frzSpMWN2yU.jpg",
          "medium": "https://d1c0x5rkl7k63i.cloudfront.net/upload/drug/brand/image/3x6d529VuZfustpLilKnUESN79R8Zi7kE4bUDOHGK5hNm8CZ4Z.jpg",
          "small": "https://d1c0x5rkl7k63i.cloudfront.net/upload/drug/brand/image/UCeQwxtFbWrd7l7ZjXgePfAP9rJzFro9dxSarwiOLqEE8gkdrT.jpg"
        },
        "brand_status": 1,
        "content": "Ceftolozane, tazobactam",
        "brand_info": [
          {
            "title": "Therapeutic class",
            "content": "Cephalosporins + Beta-Lactamase Inhibitors"
          },
          {
            "title": "Regulatory Classification",
            "content": "Rx"
          },
          {
            "title": "Indications",
            "content": "Treatment of complicated intra-abdominal infections caused by G(-) and G(+) microorganisms e.g. <i>Enterobacter cloacae</i>, <i>Escherichia coli</i>, <i>Klebsiella oxytoca</i>, <i>K. pneumoniae</i>, <i>Proteus mirabilis</i>, <i>Pseudomonas aeruginosa</i>, <i>Bacteroides fragilis</i>, <i>Streptococcus anginosus</i>, <i>constellatus</i> and <i>salivarius</i> in patients >18 yrs (in combination with metronidazole). Treatment of complicated urinary tract infection (UTI) including pyelonephritis with or without concurrent bacteremia caused by G(-) microorganisms e.g. <i>Escherichia coli</i>, <i>K. pneumoniae</i>, <i>Proteus mirabilis</i> and <i>Pseudomonas aeruginosa</i>. Treatment of nosocomial pneumonia, including ventilator-associated pneumonia, caused by G(-) microorganisms e.g. <i>Enterobacter cloacae</i>, <i>Escherichia coli</i>, <i>Haemophilus influenzae</i>, <i>K.</i> (Enterobacter) <i>aerogenes</i>, <i>K. oxytoca</i>, <i>K. pneumoniae</i>, <i>Proteus mirabilis</i>, <i>Pseudomonas aeruginosa</i> and <i>Serratia marcescens</i>."
          },
          {
            "title": "Dose",
            "content": "IV infusion over 1 hr. <b>Complicated intra-abdominal infections:</b> 1 g/500 mg for 4-14 days. <b>Complicated UTI including pyelonephritis:</b> 1 g/500 mg for 7 days. <b>Treatment of nosocomial pneumonia, including ventilator-associated pneumonia:</b> 2 g/1 g by for 8-14 days. <b>Complicated intra-abdominal infections and complicated UTI including pyelonephritis with renal impairment -</b> CrCl 30-50 mL/min: 500 mg/250 mg; 15-29 mL/min: 250 mg/125 mg; ESRD on hemodialysis: 500 mg/250 mg followed after 8 hrs by 100 mg/50 mg maintenance dose. <b>Nosocomial pneumonia including ventilator-associated pneumonia with renal impairment -</b> CrCl 30-50 mL/min: 1 g/500 mg; 15-29 mL/min: 500 mg/250 mg; ESRD on hemodialysis: 1.5 g/750 mg as single loading dose followed by 300 mg/150 mg maintenance dose for the remainder of the treatment period. Administer at the earliest possible time following completion of dialysis on hemodialysis days. Administer all doses every 8 hrs."
          },
          {
            "title": "Contraindications",
            "content": "Hypersensitivity to cephalosporin, penicillins, carbapenems, or other beta-lactam antibacterial drugs."
          },
          {
            "title": "Precautions",
            "content": "Patients with impaired renal function. Consider <i>Clostridium difficile</i>-associated diarrhea (CDAD) in patients who present with diarrhea and discontinue therapy in such circumstances. Childn and adolescents < 18 yrs. Pregnancy and lactation.",
            "pregnancy_risk": null
          },
          {
            "title": "Side Effects",
            "content": "Nausea, diarrhea, headache, pyrexia, constipation, insomnia, vomiting, hypokalemia, elevated ALT and AST, anemia, thrombocytosis, abdominal pain, anxiety, dizziness, hypotension, atrial fibrillation, rash, infusion site reactions, atrial fibrillation."
          },
          {
            "title": "Packaging",
            "content": "Powder for injection (IV) 1 g/500 mg (P4,152.00/vial)."
          }
        ],
        "company": {

          "name": "Merck Sharp & Dohme (I.A.) LLC"
        },

  {
    "id": 4465,
    "detail": {
      "success": true,
      "data": {
        "id": 4465,
        "brand_name": "Zestril",
        "brand_suffix": null,
        "brand_shot": {
          "original": "https://d1c0x5rkl7k63i.cloudfront.net/upload/drug/brand/image/PfLCz5B4TbbcESKcfnMzvJGygqQNyMxFr8x7nG7wWfNdrGqiBd.jpg",
          "medium": "https://d1c0x5rkl7k63i.cloudfront.net/upload/drug/brand/image/vDGwfuLxVUtc1XiOEiDetLGk6NYcy0WSbAr1HbLqJ6RN67LTsl.jpg",
          "small": "https://d1c0x5rkl7k63i.cloudfront.net/upload/drug/brand/image/fFGgUy8FPOPloaU5b4eFl0DAsLd2a1qdD523i0UW8ssCXAMFhI.jpg"
        },
        "brand_status": 1,
        "content": "Lisinopril",
        "brand_info": [
          {
            "title": "Therapeutic class",
            "content": "ACE Inhibitors"
          },
          {
            "title": "Regulatory Classification",
            "content": "Rx"
          },
          {
            "title": "Indications",
            "content": "Treatment of hypertension (HTN) and in renovascular HTN. Management of congestive heart failure (CHF) as an adjunct treatment with diuretics. Treatment of hemodynamically stable patients within 24 hrs of an acute myocardial infarction (MI) to prevent subsequent development of left ventricular dysfunction or heart failure. To reduce urinary albumin excretion rate in normotensive insulin-dependent and hypertensive non-insulindependent diabetes mellitus (DM) patients with incipient nephropathy."
          },
          {
            "title": "Dose",
            "content": "<b>Adults - HTN:</b> Initially 10 mg. Maintenance dose: 20 mg as single daily dose. Max 80 mg daily. Patients on diuretics: Initially 5 mg. Renovascular HTN: Initially 2.5 or 5 mg. <b>Renal impairment – CrCl 31-80 mL/min:</b> Initially 5-10 mg/day. <b>10-30 mL/min:</b> 2.5-5 mg/day; <b>&lt;10 mL/min/1.73 m<sup>2</sup>:</b> 2.5 mg/day. Dosage may be titrated until BP is controlled or to max of 40 mg daily. <b>CHF:</b> Initially 2.5 mg once daily, may be increased by increments not &gt;10 mg, at intervals of not &lt;2 weeks up to max 35 mg once daily. <b>Acute MI:</b> 5 mg once daily, followed by 5 mg after 24 hrs, 10 mg after 48 hrs, then 10 mg once daily thereafter. <b>Patients with low systolic BP (&lt;120 mmHg):</b> 2.5 mg initially or during the first 3 days; if hypotension (SBP &le;100 mmHg) occurs, maintenance of 5 mg/day may be reduced to 2.5 mg. <b>Renal complications of DM:</b> 10 mg once daily, may be increased to 20 mg once daily. <b>Childn and adolescents 6-16 yrs – &ge;50 kg BW:</b> 5 mg once daily. Max 40 mg daily; <b>20-50 kg BW:</b> 2.5 mg. Max 20 mg daily."
          },
          {
            "title": "Contraindications",
            "content": "Hypersensitivity. Patients with history of anaphylactic/anaphylactoid reactions or angioedema from previous treatment with ACE inhibitors. Concomitant use of aliskiren-containing drugs in patients with DM (type 1 or 2) or with moderate to severe renal impairment (GFR &lt;60 mL/min/1.73 m<sup>2</sup>). Pregnancy (2<sup>nd</sup> or 3<sup>rd</sup> trimesters)."
          },
          {
            "title": "Precautions",
            "content": "Patients with vol-depletion, CHF with or without renal insufficiency, IHD, or cerebrovascular disease; aortic stenosis or hypertrophic cardiomyopathy; SBP &le;100 mmHg or cardiogenic shock. Dual blockade of RAAS. Major surgery or during anesthesia. Renal impairment and dialysis. Withhold treatment during desensitization treatment (Hymenoptera venom). ACE-inhibitor induced cough. May impair ability to drive and operate machinery. Black patients. Childn &lt;6yrs or in childn with severe renal impairment (GFR &lt;30 mL/min/1.73 m<sup>2</sup>). Elderly. Lactation.",
            "pregnancy_risk": null
          },
          {
            "title": "Side Effects",
            "content": "Dizziness, headache; orthostatic effects (including hyotension); diarrhea, vomiting; cough, renal dysfunction."
          },
          {
            "title": "Drug Interactions",
            "content": "Temsirolimus, sirolimus, everolimus, racecadotril. Diuretics, antidiabetics. Potassium supplements, potassium-sparing diuretics, potassium-containing salt substitutes. Lithium, gold, NSAIDs."
          },
          {
            "title": "Packaging",
            "content": "Tablet 5 mg, 10 mg, 20 mg."
          }
        ],
        "company": {
          "id": 3866,
          "name": "DKSH Market Expansion Services Philippines, Inc."
        },

  {
    "id": 4583,
    "detail": {
      "success": true,
      "data": {
        "id": 4583,
        "brand_name": "Zetlow-10",
        "brand_suffix": null,
        "brand_shot": {
          "original": "https://d1c0x5rkl7k63i.cloudfront.net/upload/drug/brand/image/IgGPaLGoAF3MWdFoUU0ehk1SL9F1f8R86dPERSZmWmEFNNZOSL.jpg",
          "medium": "https://d1c0x5rkl7k63i.cloudfront.net/upload/drug/brand/image/TDORuhAamJdFJGiapA1605Uk5DhaIcabKRUMb9hm9WSDoTKFcL.jpg",
          "small": "https://d1c0x5rkl7k63i.cloudfront.net/upload/drug/brand/image/OE4O1EZ5kKUFaSBUyTwqpx4ctCy9kFNtJiMYmduRyUvSG3XgSS.jpg"
        },
        "brand_status": 1,
        "content": "Ezetimibe",
        "brand_info": [
          {
            "title": "Therapeutic class",
            "content": "Selective Cholesterol-Absorption Inhibitors"
          },
          {
            "title": "Regulatory Classification",
            "content": "Rx"
          },
          {
            "title": "Indications",
            "content": "Adjunctive therapy to diet for reduction of elevated total cholesterol (total-C), LDL-C, apolipoprotein B (Apo B), and non-HDL-C in patients with primary (heterozygous familial and non-familial) hyperlipidemia. In combination with HMG-CoA reductase inhibitor as adjunctive therapy to diet for reduction of elevated total-C, LDL-C, Apo B, and non-HDL-C in patients with primary hyperlipidemia. In combination with fenofibrate as adjunctive therapy to diet for reduction of elevated total-C, LDL-C, Apo B, and non-HDL-C in adult patients with mixed hyperlipidemia. In combination with atorvastatin or simvastatin for reduction of elevated total-C and LDL-C levels in patients with homozygous familial hypercholesterolemia as adjunct to other lipid-lowering treatments (e.g. LDL apheresis)."
          },
          {
            "title": "Dose",
            "content": "10 mg once daily. Taken with or without food. <b>Concomitant lipid-lowering therapy</b>: Take ezetimibe tab at the same time as statin or fenofibrate. <b>Coadministration with bile acid sequestrant</b>: &ge;2 hrs before or &ge;4 hrs after administration of bile acid sequestrant."
          },
          {
            "title": "Contraindications",
            "content": "Hypersensitivity. Patients with active liver disease or unexplained persistent elevations in hepatic transaminase levels. Pregnancy and lactation."
          },
          {
            "title": "Precautions",
            "content": "Concomitant use with statins or fenofibrates. Renal and hepatic impairment. Childn. Elderly.  <b><a href=\"#\" id=\"has_preg_risk\">Pregnancy Risk</a></b>: Category C.",
            "pregnancy_risk": {
              "header": "Pregnancy Risk Category by the US FDA:",
              "categories": [
                {
                  "title": "A",
                  "content": "Adequate and compelling studies in humans do not show any evidence that the product poses a risk to the baby in the womb."
                },
                {
                  "title": "B",
                  "content": "Adequate and compelling studies in animals do not show a risk to the baby, but there are not enough compelling studies in humans. Otherwise, animal studies show a risk but studies on humans do not show any."
                },
                {
                  "title": "C",
                  "content": "Adequate and compelling studies in animals show a risk to the baby, but there are not enough compelling studies in humans. However, the product may be used in pregnant women in certain situations despite potential risks."
                },
                {
                  "title": "D",
                  "content": "Adequate and compelling studies in humans show a risk to the baby. However, the product may be used in pregnant women in certain situations despite the potential risks."
                },
                {
                  "title": "X",
                  "content": "Adequate and compelling studies in animals or humans show a risk to the baby in the womb. Also, use or experience in humans shows a risk. The danger of using the product in pregnant women clearly outweighs its benefits."
                }
              ]
            }
          },
          {
            "title": "Side Effects",
            "content": "Diarrhea; fatigue; influenza, sinusitis, URTI; arthralgia, back pain, myalgia, pain in extremity. Anaphylaxis, angioedema, rash, urticaria, erythema multiforme, myalgia, elevated creatinine phosphokinase, myopathy/rhabdomyolysis; elevations in liver transaminases; hepatitis, nausea, dizziness, paresthesia, depression, headache, cholelithiasis, cholecystitis."
          },
          {
            "title": "Drug Interactions",
            "content": "Ciclosporin, fenofibrate, cholestyramine, coumarin anticoagulants."
          },
          {
            "title": "Packaging",
            "content": "Tablet 10 mg (P24.25)."
          }
        ],
        "company": {
          "id": 1246,
          "name": "Torrent Pharma Philippines, Inc."
        },

  {
    "id": 543,
    "detail": {
      "success": true,
      "data": {
        "id": 543,
        "brand_name": "Ziac",
        "brand_suffix": null,
        "brand_shot": {
          "original": "https://d1c0x5rkl7k63i.cloudfront.net/upload/drug/brand/image/Q6NKYSzRgrqeNvkMJdW8yVf1Cw4w1aSCMn68mDm2KMBuPD4BcX.jpg",
          "medium": "https://d1c0x5rkl7k63i.cloudfront.net/upload/drug/brand/image/0D12pJjXXXwzXjts9bf95z7uqrP1ZzsblLxILXrZIBISOBEm7H.jpg",
          "small": "https://d1c0x5rkl7k63i.cloudfront.net/upload/drug/brand/image/uF81tSc6NULRU7GdHEweu4OSSI3oEQygn8JVQid56sfYksoEjF.jpg"
        },
        "brand_status": 1,
        "content": "Bisoprolol fumarate, hydrochlorothiazide",
        "brand_info": [
          {
            "title": "Therapeutic class",
            "content": "Beta Blockers (Antihypertensives)"
          },
          {
            "title": "Regulatory Classification",
            "content": "Rx"
          },
          {
            "title": "Indications",
            "content": "Treatment of essential hypertension."
          },
          {
            "title": "Dose",
            "content": "<b>Adults:</b> 2.5 mg/6.25 mg once daily. If inadequate, increase to one tab 5 mg/6.25 mg once daily, and if response is still inadequate, increase to one tab of 10 mg/6.25 mg once daily."
          },
          {
            "title": "Contraindications",
            "content": "Hypersensitivity. Patients with acute heart failure decompensation requiring IV inotropic therapy, cardiogenic shock, 2<sup>nd</sup> or 3<sup>rd</sup> degree AV block, sick sinus syndrome, SA block, symptomatic bradycardia, severe bronchial asthma, severe forms of PAOD or Raynaud’s syndrome, untreated pheochromocytoma, severe renal impairment (CrCl < 30 mL/min), severe hepatic impairment, metabolic acidosis, refractory hypokalemia."
          },
          {
            "title": "Precautions",
            "content": "Avoid abrupt withdrawal. Patients with accompanying heart failure, DM showing large fluctuations in blood glucose values (symptoms of hypoglycemia can be masked), strict fasting, 1<sup>st</sup> degree AV block, Prinzmetal angina, PAOD, hypovolemia, impaired liver function, present or history of psoriasis. May mask symptoms of thyrotoxicosis. Avoid in patients with obstructive airway diseases. If necessary, gradually withdraw for 24 hrs prior to anesthesia. Photosensitivity.  May increase risk of non-melanoma skin cancer (NMSC). Limit exposure to sunlight and UV rays. Fluid and electrolyte disturbances in long term use. May increase risk of gout attacks in patients with hyperuricemia. Worsened metabolic alkalosis. Acute cholelithiasis. Discontinue if acute respiratory distress syndrome occurs. Monitor serum electrolytes, creatinine, urea, serum lipids, uric acid, and blood glucose during long-term therapy.  May impair ability to drive or operate machinery. Childn. Pregnancy and lactation.",
            "pregnancy_risk": null
          },
          {
            "title": "Side Effects",
            "content": "Dizziness, headache; feeling of coldness or numbness in extremities; nausea, vomiting, diarrhea, constipation; fatigue."
          },
          {
            "title": "Drug Interactions",
            "content": "Lithium, verapamil, diltiazem, clonidine, digitalis glycosides, NSAIDs, isoprenaline, dobutamine, norepinephrine, methyldopa, moxonidine,  rilmenidine, nifedipine, amlodipine, captopril, enalapril, barbiturates, phenothiazines, TCAs, quinidine, disopyramide, lidocaine, phenytoin, flecainide, propafenone, amiodarone, hydroquinidine, quinidine, disopyramide, parasympathomimetic agents, uric-acid-lowering agents, sotalol, dofetilide, ibutilide, astemizole, erythromycin, halofantrine, pentamidine, sparfloxacin, terfenadine, vincamine, topical beta-blockers, epinephrine, corticosteroids, ACTH, carbenoxolone, amphotericin B, furosemide, laxatives, cholestyramine, colestipol, mefloquine, MAOIs (except MAO-B), rifampicin, ergot derivatives. Diabetic and anesthetic agents."
          },
          {
            "title": "Packaging",
            "content": "Film-coated tablet 2.5 mg/6.25 mg, 5 mg/6.25 mg, 10 mg/6.25 mg."
          }
        ],

          "original": "https://d1c0x5rkl7k63i.cloudfront.net/upload/drug/brand/image/4bYfeZJbjkfz9cGhMw5bFDWNEwpoZHlag5RQvkPRiTOJBqxBRU.jpg",
          "medium": "https://d1c0x5rkl7k63i.cloudfront.net/upload/drug/brand/image/f6kqN80NUPU2iLEddmBxSiBUSPwwtiiR6UhG9MItti38fLnTBZ.jpg",
          "small": "https://d1c0x5rkl7k63i.cloudfront.net/upload/drug/brand/image/RjTcXAUKkRNZ4DA6XQ5qlZt2zIimtiabYm8gxyrqy0kyPm26VR.jpg"
        },
        "brand_status": 1,
        "content": "Olanzapine",
        "brand_info": [
          {
            "title": "Therapeutic class",
            "content": "Atypical Antipsychotics"
          },
          {
            "title": "Regulatory Classification",
            "content": "Rx"
          },
          {
            "title": "Indications",
            "content": "Treatment for schizophrenia; moderate to severe manic episodes. Prevention of recurrence in patients with bipolar disorder whose manic episodes have responded to olanzapine treatment."
          },
          {
            "title": "Dose",
            "content": "Daily dosage may be individually adjusted within the range of 5-20 mg/day at intervals of >24 hrs. <b>Adults - Schizophrenia:</b> 10 mg/day. <b>Manic episodes:</b> Initially 15 mg once daily as monotherapy or 10 mg daily in combination therapy. <b>Prevention of recurrence in bipolar disorder:</b> Initially 10 mg/day. Continue at the same dose of treatment for manic episodes. If new manic, mixed, or depressive episode occurs, olanzapine should be continued (with dose optimization as needed), with supplementary therapy to treat mood symptoms. <b>Elderly &ge;65 yrs and patients with renal and/or hepatic impairment:</b> Initially 5 mg/day. <b>Moderate hepatic insufficiency (e.g. cirrhosis, Child-Pugh class A or B):</b> Initially 5 mg, only increased with caution. Place tab immediately in the mouth and allow it to disperse in saliva to be easily swallowed or immediately dispersed in a full glass of water or other beverages (e.g. orange or apple juice, milk, coffee) before administration."
          },
          {
            "title": "Contraindications",
            "content": "Hypersensitivity. Patients with known risk of narrow-angle glaucoma."
          },
          {
            "title": "Precautions",
            "content": "Avoid abrupt withdrawal. Not indicated for dementia-related psychosis and/or behavioral disturbances; dopamine agonist-associated psychosis in patients with Parkinson’s disease. Patients with prostatic hypertrophy, paralytic ileus, and related conditions; ALT and/or AST, with signs and symptoms of hepatic impairment, pre-existing conditions associated with limited hepatic functional reserve, or treated with hepatotoxic drugs; neutropenia, neutropenia-causing drugs, history of bone marrow depression, radiation therapy or chemotherapy, hyper eosinophilic conditions, myeloproliferative disease; congenital long QT prolongation, CHF, heart hypertrophy, hypokalemia, hypomagnesemia; history of seizures or other conditions that potentially lower the seizure threshold; phenylketonuria. Concomitant use with other centrally acting medicines and alcohol. Consider lowering the initial dose when >1 factor which might result in slower metabolism (e.g. female, geriatric age, non-smoking status) is present. Monitor BP regularly of patients >65 yrs, signs and symptoms of hyperglycemia and/or development or exacerbation of diabetes, weight, lipid alterations, anticholinergic activity, QT prolongation, VTE, tardive dyskinesia, postural hypotension, sudden cardiac death. Discontinue treatment when hepatitis, hepatocellular, cholestatic, mixed liver injury or signs and symptoms of NMS occur. Childn and adolescents < 18 yrs. Women of childbearing potential. Pregnancy and lactation.",
            "pregnancy_risk": null
          },
          {
            "title": "Side Effects",
            "content": "Sedation, hypersomnia, lethargy, somnolence, weight gain, eosinophilia, elevated prolactin, cholesterol, glucose, and triglyceride levels, decreased total bilirubin, glucosuria, increased appetite, dizziness, akathisia, parkinsonism, dyskinesia, orthostatic hypotension, anticholinergic effects, ALT/AST elevations, rash, asthenia, fatigue, edema."
          },
          {
            "title": "Drug Interactions",
            "content": "CYP1A2 inducers (e.g. smoking, carbamazepine) and inhibitors (e.g. fluvoxamine). Activated charcoal. Direct and indirect dopamine agonists. Alcohol, CNS depressants, anti-Parkisonian medications. QT-prolonging agents."
          },
          {
            "title": "Packaging",
            "content": "Orodispersible tablet 5 mg (P42.00), 10 mg (P56.00)."
          }
        ],
        "company": {
          "id": 1609,
          "name": "Emcure Pharma Philippines Inc."
        },

        "brand_suffix": null,
        "brand_shot": {
          "original": "https://d1c0x5rkl7k63i.cloudfront.net/upload/drug/brand/image/srz2f2fjZGn0yM34SoOheTtmTuCnuBfVdcbwek7NmYMXpfacxj.jpg",
          "medium": "https://d1c0x5rkl7k63i.cloudfront.net/upload/drug/brand/image/w62PEXGjr4tYT56JDlZdVSpSFS2J9ZHcXGGZJNvyg33eQW6gjo.jpg",
          "small": "https://d1c0x5rkl7k63i.cloudfront.net/upload/drug/brand/image/EpbpLQxW8utYR3pDaMwpuVW1uhKI6ROU3ONipgHSxr6d44nWwG.jpg"
        },
        "brand_status": 1,
        "content": "Sildenafil citrate",
        "brand_info": [
          {
            "title": "Therapeutic class",
            "content": "Drugs Used in Erectile Dysfunction"
          },
          {
            "title": "Regulatory Classification",
            "content": "Rx"
          },
          {
            "title": "Indications",
            "content": "Management of erectile dysfunction."
          },
          {
            "title": "Dose",
            "content": "50 mg approx 1 hr before sexual activity, taken before or after meals. May be increased to max 100 mg or decreased to 25 mg, once per day. Max 100 mg. <b>Hepatic and severe renal impairment:</b> 25 mg. <b>Patients using other drugs</b> - Ritonavir: Max single dose 25 mg in a 48-hr period; CYP3A4 inhibitors (e.g. erythromycin, saquinavir, ketoconazole, itraconazole): 25 mg."
          },
          {
            "title": "Contraindications",
            "content": "Hypersensitivity. Concomitant use with organic nitrates or nitrites. Newborn. Childn. Women."
          },
          {
            "title": "Precautions",
            "content": "Consider CV status of patient. Patients with left ventricular outflow obstruction, severely impaired autonomic control of BP, resting hypotension (BP &lt;90/50) or HTN (BP &gt;170/110), cardiac failure or CAD, retinitis pigmentosa; suffered from MI, stroke, or life-threatening arrhythmia within the last 6 months; anatomical deformation, conditions which may predispose to priapism (e.g. sickle cell anemia, multiple myeloma, leukemia), bleeding disorders or active peptic ulceration. Concomitant use with ritonavir, alpha-blocker.",
            "pregnancy_risk": null
          },
          {
            "title": "Side Effects",
            "content": "Headache, flushing, dyspepsia, dizziness, vomiting, diarrhea, hypotension, tachycardia syncope, epistaxis, nasal congestion, rash, eye pain and redness, muscle or back pain, prolonged erection or priapism. UTI, CV hemorrhage, TIA."
          },
          {
            "title": "Drug Interactions",
            "content": "Ketoconazole, erythromycin, cimetidine, saquinavir, ritonavir, alpha-blocker therapy."
          },
          {
            "title": "Packaging",
            "content": "Film-coated tablet 50 mg, 100 mg."
          }
        ],
        "company": {

        },
