  {
    "id": 2918,
    "detail": {
      "success": true,
      "data": {
        "id": 2918,
        "brand_name": "Zynapse",
        "brand_suffix": null,
        "brand_shot": {
          "original": "https://d1c0x5rkl7k63i.cloudfront.net/upload/drug/brand/image/lsk4ms9cxFQyPZdFrdjDC4JKOBPhxb4j5aIbGYswiKrUnsiI1F.jpg",
          "medium": "https://d1c0x5rkl7k63i.cloudfront.net/upload/drug/brand/image/o3rUtgtWroBk2l8cA6l9NexIsBwLRnVFl1na5evd0Tf3ymAzJF.jpg",
          "small": "https://d1c0x5rkl7k63i.cloudfront.net/upload/drug/brand/image/GOVa1nPE98tDklKTPls4ZPCRs0Hf67NaBnF7hXfd17EX3Yphsb.jpg"
        },
        "brand_status": 1,
        "content": "Citicoline (as sodium)",
        "brand_info": [
          {
            "title": "Therapeutic class",
            "content": "CNS Stimulants, Neurotonics, & Neurotrophics"
          },
          {
            "title": "Regulatory Classification",
            "content": "Rx"
          },
          {
            "title": "Indications",
            "content": "For cerebrovascular accidents, in acute recovery phase, in signs and symptoms of cerebral vascular insufficiency and in cranial traumatism and their sequelae. <b>Cap/Tab/Oral drops</b>: Cerebrovascular disease, head trauma of varying disease, cognitive disorders of diverse etiology, Parkinson’s disease."
          },
          {
            "title": "Dose",
            "content": "<b>Adults – Cap</b>: 1 cap once or twice daily. <b>Tab</b>: 1 tab once or twice daily. <b>Oral drops</b>: 1-2 mL twice or thrice daily. <b>Solution for injection 125 mg/mL (Zynapse 500)</b>: 1-2 injections daily; <b>250 mg/mL (Zynapse 1000)</b>: 1 injection daily. Dose may be adjusted based on the seriousness of the disease, given by IM, IV every 3-5 mins, and IV drop perfusion (dripping speed 40-60 drops/min)."
          },
          {
            "title": "Contraindications",
            "content": "Hypertonia of the parasympathetic."
          },
          {
            "title": "Precautions",
            "content": "In case of persistent intracranial hemorrhage, very slow administration (30 drops/min) is recommended. Provoke an increase of cerebral blood flow (large doses). Do not administer in conjunction with meclofenoxate (clofenoxate). Pregnancy and lactation.",
            "pregnancy_risk": null
          },
          {
            "title": "Side Effects",
            "content": "Parasympathetic, fleeting and discrete hypotensor effects."
          },
          {
            "title": "Drug Interactions",
            "content": "Levodopa, meclofenoxate."
          },
          {
            "title": "Packaging",
            "content": "Capsule 500 mg (P65.82). Tablet 1 g (P93.20). Adult oral drops 100 mg/mL x 15 mL (P239.20). Solution for injection (IV/IM) 125 mg/mL (Zynapse 500) x 4 mL (P129.76), 250 mg/mL (Zynapse 1000) x 4 mL (P221.65)."
          }
        ],

        "brand_info": [
          {
            "title": "Therapeutic class",
            "content": "Cholinesterase Inhibitors"
          },
          {
            "title": "Regulatory Classification",
            "content": "Rx"
          },
          {
            "title": "Indications",
            "content": "Symptomatic treatment of mild to moderately severe Alzheimer's dementia."
          },
          {
            "title": "Dose",
            "content": "<b>Adults and elderly</b>: Initially 5 mg once daily at bedtime, maintained for at least 1 month. May be increased to max 10 mg/day after clinical assessment."
          },
          {
            "title": "Contraindications",
            "content": "Hypersensitivity."
          },
          {
            "title": "Precautions",
            "content": "Patients with severe Alzheimer’s disease, other types of dementia or memory impairment (age-related cognitive decline), sick-sinus syndrome, other supraventricular cardiac conditions (SA or AV block), history of asthma or obstructive pulmonary disease, severe hepatic impairment. Patients at risk for developing GI ulcers. Anesthesia (succinylcholine-type). May induce bladder outflow obstruction, EPS. May impair ability to drive and operate machines. Childn. Pregnancy and lactation.",
            "pregnancy_risk": null
          },
          {
            "title": "Side Effects",
            "content": "Diarrhea, nausea. Common cold, anorexia, hallucinations, agitation, aggressive behavior, abnormal dreams and nightmares, syncope, dizziness, insomnia, vomiting, abdominal disturbance."
          },
          {
            "title": "Drug Interactions",
            "content": "CYP3A4 and 2D6 inhibitors (e.g. ketoconazole, itraconazole, quinidine, erythromycin, fluoxetine) and inducers (e.g. rifampicin, phenytoin, carbamazepine and alcohol), anticholinergics, succinylcholine, other neuromuscular blocking agents, cholinergic agonists, beta-blockers."
          },
          {
            "title": "Packaging",
            "content": "Film-coated tablet 5 mg (P38.39), 10 mg (P58.04)."
          }
        ],

        "content": "Levocetirizine HCl",
        "brand_info": [
          {
            "title": "Therapeutic class",
            "content": "Second Generation Antihistamines"
          },
          {
            "title": "Regulatory Classification",
            "content": "Rx"
          },
          {
            "title": "Indications",
            "content": "For the relief of nasal and ocular symptoms of seasonal and perennial allergic rhinitis, and chronic idiopathic urticaria."
          },
          {
            "title": "Dose",
            "content": "<b>Adults and childn &ge;12 yrs:</b> 2 tsp (10 mL). <b>Childn 6-11 yrs:</b> 1 tsp (5 mL); <b>6 months to 5 yrs:</b> 1.25 mg (2.5 mL). All doses are taken once daily in the evening. <b>Renal impairment</b> - CrCl 50-&ge;80 mL/min: 5 mg once daily; 30-49 mL/min: 5 mg once every 2 days; &lt;30 mL/min: 5 mg once every 3 days."
          },
          {
            "title": "Contraindications",
            "content": "Hypersensitivity. Patients with severe renal impairment (CrCl &lt;10 mL/min) or ESRD and undergoing dialysis. Patients with galactose intolerance, Lapp lactase deficiency, or glucose-galactose malabsorption. Childn 6 months-11 yrs with renal impairment."
          },
          {
            "title": "Precautions",
            "content": "Concurrent use with alcohol or other CNS depressants. Patients with epilepsy and those at risk of convulsions; predisposing factors of urinary retention e.g. spinal cord lesion and prostatic hyperplasia. May impair ability to drive or operate machines. Childn &lt;2 yrs. Pregnancy and lactation.",
            "pregnancy_risk": null
          },
          {
            "title": "Side Effects",
            "content": "Headache, somnolence, mouth dryness, fatigue. Hypersensitivity (e.g. anaphylaxis), aggression, agitation, convulsion, visual disturbances, palpitation, dyspnea, nausea, hepatitis, angioneurotic edema, fixed drug eruption, pruritus, rash, and urticaria."
          },
          {
            "title": "Drug Interactions",
            "content": "CNS depressants, alcohol, and theophylline."
          },
          {
            "title": "Packaging",
            "content": "Syrup 2.5 mg/5 mL x 60 mL (P208.88)."
          }
        ],

          },
          {
            "title": "Indications",
            "content": "Symptomatic relief of allergic conditions including rhinitis and chronic urticaria."
          },
          {
            "title": "Dose",
            "content": "<b>Adults and childn &ge;6 yrs:</b> 1 tab once daily or &frac12; tab twice daily. May be given with or without food. <b>Syr: Childn 6-11 yrs-</b> 5-10 mL (1-2 tsp) given once daily. <b>2-5 yrs:</b> Initially 2.5 mL (&frac12; tsp) once daily, may be increased to max 5 mL/day. <b>6 months -2 yrs:</b> 2.5 mL (&frac12; tsp) once daily, may be increased to max 5 mL/day (given as 2.5 mL every 12 hrs). <b>Seasonal rhinitis:</b> Dose should be given at the beginning of the hay fever season when pollens are still few. <b>Hepatic and renal impairment:</b> Dose is reduced to &frac12; of the usual daily dose."
          },
          {
            "title": "Contraindications",
            "content": "Hypersensitivity to cetirizine, hydroxyzine, piperazine derivatives or any component of formulation. Hypokalemic patients with chronic renal failure undergoing hemodialysis. Childn &lt;6 yrs with hepatic and renal impairment."
          },
          {
            "title": "Precautions",
            "content": "Antihistamine intake should be stopped several days before skin testing. May impair ability to drive or operate machines. Alcohol intake. Renal or hepatic impairment. Childn. Elderly. Pregnancy and lactation. <b><a href=\"#\" id=\"has_preg_risk\">Pregnancy Risk</a>:</b> Category B.",

            "title": "Side Effects",
            "content": "Drowsiness, recurrent acute hepatitis, irritability, insomnia, somnolence, fatigue, dry mouth, pharyngitis, dizziness, headache, abdominal pain, cough, diarrhea, epistaxis, bronchospasm, nausea, vomiting, hypersensitivity, nervousness, rashes, anaphylactic shock, aggression, hallucination, dysgeusia, dyskinesia, dystonia, memory impairment, syncope, tremor, eye pain, eye swelling, vision blurred, abnormal hepatic function, pruritis, urticaria, acute generalized exanthemotous pustulosis, arthralgia, enuresis, urinary retention, erectile dysfunction, malaise, feeling abnormal, pruritus upon withdrawal, weight increased, life-threatening hepatitis (long-term use for atopic dermatitis)."
          },

            "content": "Barbiturates, alcohol, hypnotics, opioid analgesics, sedatives, neuroleptics, and other CNS depressants."

        "content": "Cetirizine, phenylephrine HCl",
        "brand_info": [
          {
            "title": "Therapeutic class",

          },
          {
            "title": "Regulatory Classification",
            "content": "Non-Rx"
          },
          {
            "title": "Indications",
            "content": "For the relief of clogged nose, sneezing, runny and itchy nose, and watery eyes associated with allergic rhinitis."
          },
          {
            "title": "Dose",
            "content": "Childn 6-12 yrs: 5 mL (1 tsp). 2-5 yrs: 2.5 mL (&frac12; tsp). Doses taken every 12 hrs or as directed."
          },
          {
            "title": "Contraindications",
            "content": "Hypersensitivity. Patients taking medicines for depression; with high BP or severe heart diseases."
          },
          {
            "title": "Precautions",
            "content": "Kidney or liver disease. <b><a href=\"#\" id=\"has_preg_risk\">Pregnancy Risk</a>:</b> Category B for cetirizine; Category C for phenylephrine.",
            "pregnancy_risk": {
              "header": "Pregnancy Risk Category by the US FDA:",

                }
              ]
            }
          },
          {
            "title": "Side Effects",
            "content": "Palpitation, nausea, weakness, insomnia, nervousness. <b>Cetirizine</b>: CNS stimulation or depression, CV collapse with hypotension, tachycardia, dry mouth, asthenia, restlessness, ataxia, anorexia, headache, dizziness, vertigo, convulsions, anxiety, fear, hallucinations, dysphonia, dysuria, respiratory difficulty, nose bleeding, pharyngitis, and pallor. <b>Phenylephrine</b>: Reflex bradycardia, arrhythmia, ischemia, extrasystoles, tachycardia, vomiting, headache, paresthesia, tremor, anxiety, agitation, excitability, urinary retention (in males), dyspnea, pulmonary edema, blanching of skin, pallor, piloerection, HTN, hypertensive crisis, cerebral hemorrhage, ventricular arrhythmia, MI."
          },
          {
            "title": "Drug Interactions",
            "content": "Alcohol and tobacco use. Blood thinners (e.g. warfarin, aspirin), other anti-allergic drugs (diphenhydramine), anti-depressants (e.g. alprazolam, amitriptyline, amoxapine, clomipramine, doxepin), anticonvulsants (e.g. fosphenytoin, levetiracetam, phenytoin, valproic acid), HIV medications (e.g. lopinavir, ritonavir, saquinavir), antipsychotics (e.g. chlorpromazine, clozapine), antidiabetics (e.g. metformin, glimepiride, glipizide), digoxin, grapefruit, and St. John’s Wort."
          },

            "title": "Packaging",
            "content": "Syrup 5 mg/5 mg per 5 mL x 60 mL (Banana flavor) (P268.80)."

            "title": "Therapeutic class",
            "content": "Statins"
          },
          {
            "title": "Regulatory Classification",
            "content": "Rx"
          },
          {
            "title": "Indications",
            "content": "Adjunct to diet to reduce elevated total cholesterol, LDL-C, apolipoprotein B, nonHDL-C, and triglycerides and to increase HDL-C in adults with primary hyperlipidemia or mixed dyslipidemia, homozygous familial hyperlipidemia; in adolescent boys and girls (at least 1 yr postmenarche) 10-17 yrs with heterozygous familial hypercholesterolemia; hypertriglyceridemia, primary dysbetalipoproteinemia (type III hyperlipoproteinemia), slowing the progression of atherosclerosis. Primary prevention of cardiovascular (CV) disease."
          },
          {
            "title": "Dose",
            "content": "Initial dose: 10-20 mg. Usual dose range: 5-40 mg once daily. <b>Asians and patients requiring less aggressive LDL-C reductions or who have predisposing factors for myopathy</b>: Initially 5 mg daily. <b>Homozygous familial hypercholesterolemia</b>: Initially 20 mg. Patients who have not achieved goal LDL-C at 20 mg: 40 mg. <b>Heterozygous familial hypercholesterolemia in childn 10-17 yrs</b>: Individualized dosage. Usual dose range: 5-20 mg/day. Max 20 mg/day. <b>Patients taking ciclosporin</b>: Not to exceed 5 mg once daily. <b>Patients with severe renal impairment (CrCl &lt;30 mL/min/1.73 m<sup>2</sup>) not on hemodialysis or co-administration with gemfibrozil, lopinavir and ritonavir, atazanavir and ritonavir</b>: Initially 5 mg once daily. Max 10 mg once daily."
          },
          {
            "title": "Contraindications",
            "content": "Hypersensitivity. Patients with active liver disease. Pregnancy and lactation."
          },
          {
            "title": "Precautions",
            "content": "Patients with predisposing factors for myopathy (e.g. elderly &ge;65 yrs, inadequately treated hypothyroidism, renal impairment), large alcohol consumption, history of liver disease, severe renal impairment. May cause rhabdomyolysis with acute renal failure secondary to myoglobinuria, proteinuria or hematuria. Perform liver function test prior to, and 12 weeks after, initiation of therapy and any elevation of dose. Concomitant use with other lipid-lowering agents, cyclosporin, fibrates, niacin, gemfibrozil, lopinavir/ritonavir, or atazanavir/ritonavir, colchicine, ketoconazole, spironolactone, cimetidine. Discontinue if significant elevation of creatinine kinase levels occurs or myopathy develops. Asian. Elderly. Childn &lt;10 yrs. <b><a href=\"#\" id=\"has_preg_risk\">Pregnancy Risk</a></b>: Category X.",
            "pregnancy_risk": {
              "header": "Pregnancy Risk Category by the US FDA:",
              "categories": [
                {
                  "title": "A",
                  "content": "Adequate and compelling studies in humans do not show any evidence that the product poses a risk to the baby in the womb."
                },
                {
                  "title": "B",
                  "content": "Adequate and compelling studies in animals do not show a risk to the baby, but there are not enough compelling studies in humans. Otherwise, animal studies show a risk but studies on humans do not show any."
                },
                {
                  "title": "C",
                  "content": "Adequate and compelling studies in animals show a risk to the baby, but there are not enough compelling studies in humans. However, the product may be used in pregnant women in certain situations despite potential risks."
                },
                {
                  "title": "D",
                  "content": "Adequate and compelling studies in humans show a risk to the baby. However, the product may be used in pregnant women in certain situations despite the potential risks."
                },
                {
                  "title": "X",
                  "content": "Adequate and compelling studies in animals or humans show a risk to the baby in the womb. Also, use or experience in humans shows a risk. The danger of using the product in pregnant women clearly outweighs its benefits."
                }
              ]
            }
          },
          {
            "title": "Side Effects",
            "content": "Rhabdomyolysis with myoglobinuria and acute renal failure and myopathy, liver enzyme abnormalities, myalgia, constipation, abdominal pain, nausea, headache, asthenia, diarrhea, dyspepsia, back pain, flu syndrome, proteinuria, arthralgia, fatal and non-fatal hepatic failure, jaundice, thrombocytopenia, depression, sleep disorders and gynecomastia symptomatic elevation of serum transaminase and creatinine phosphokinase levels."
          },
          {
            "title": "Drug Interactions",
            "content": "Other lipid-lowering agents, ciclosporin, gemfibrozil, protease inhibitors (e.g. lopinavir or ritonavir), itraconazole, coumarin anticoagulants, warfarin, antacids containing aluminum and magnesium hydroxide, erythromycin, ethinyl estradiol and norgestrel, niacin, fenofibrate, colchicine."
          },
          {

            "content": "Film-coated tablet 5 mg (P19.75), 10 mg (P23.00), 20 mg (P33.00), 40 mg (P39.75)."

          "medium": "https://d1c0x5rkl7k63i.cloudfront.net/upload/drug/brand/image/fWwIApB9iNmfWwQnCb434UpS21yICXLsQlI7Z9x5e1Lpbm8WLK.jpg",
          "small": "https://d1c0x5rkl7k63i.cloudfront.net/upload/drug/brand/image/ZmHKgJ4hUerUOll57DqBgusMI6EuZozVIkVvWGZ5iZMeRjwETX.jpg"
        },
        "brand_status": 1,
        "content": "Divalproex sodium",
        "brand_info": [
          {
            "title": "Therapeutic class",
            "content": "Fatty Acid Derivatives"
          },
          {
            "title": "Regulatory Classification",
            "content": "Rx"
          },
          {
            "title": "Indications",
            "content": "Seizures: simple grand mal, myoclonic, complex partial, complex absence seizures. Manic episodes associated with bipolar disorder. Maintenance treatment of bipolar disorder, aggression in mental retardation, impulsive aggression in cluster B personality disorder, short-term treatment of agitation in dementia."
          },
          {
            "title": "Dose",
            "content": "<b>Epilepsy:</b> 10-15 mg/kg/day; increased by 5-10 mg/kg/day for optimal clinical response. <b>Mania:</b> 750 mg daily in divided doses. <b>Migraine:</b> 250 mg twice daily."
          },
          {
            "title": "Contraindications",
            "content": "Hypersensitivity. Hepatic disease or hepatic dysfunction."
          },
          {
            "title": "Precautions",
            "content": "Hepatic dysfunction, thrombocytopenia, hyperammonemia, patients taking other CNS depressants. Alter thyroid function tests and urine ketone test. <b><a href=\"#\" id=\"has_preg_risk\">Pregnancy Risk</a>:</b> Category C.",
            "pregnancy_risk": {
              "header": "Pregnancy Risk Category by the US FDA:",
              "categories": [
                {
                  "title": "A",
                  "content": "Adequate and compelling studies in humans do not show any evidence that the product poses a risk to the baby in the womb."
                },
                {
                  "title": "B",
                  "content": "Adequate and compelling studies in animals do not show a risk to the baby, but there are not enough compelling studies in humans. Otherwise, animal studies show a risk but studies on humans do not show any."
                },
                {
                  "title": "C",
                  "content": "Adequate and compelling studies in animals show a risk to the baby, but there are not enough compelling studies in humans. However, the product may be used in pregnant women in certain situations despite potential risks."
                },
                {
                  "title": "D",
                  "content": "Adequate and compelling studies in humans show a risk to the baby. However, the product may be used in pregnant women in certain situations despite the potential risks."
                },
                {
                  "title": "X",
                  "content": "Adequate and compelling studies in animals or humans show a risk to the baby in the womb. Also, use or experience in humans shows a risk. The danger of using the product in pregnant women clearly outweighs its benefits."
                }
              ]
            }
          },
          {
            "title": "Drug Interactions",
            "content": "Phenytoin, carbamazepine, phenobarbital (or primidone), alcohol, aspirin, clonazepam, ethosuximide, felbamate, lamotrigine, lithium, warfarin."
          },
          {
            "title": "Packaging",
            "content": "Film-coated tablet 250 mg (P18.86), 500 mg (P32.00)."
          }
        ],
        "company": {
          "id": 844,
          "name": "Zuneca Incorporated"
        },

  {
    "id": 1408,
    "detail": {
      "success": true,
      "data": {
        "id": 1408,
        "brand_name": "Zyster",
        "brand_suffix": null,
        "brand_shot": {
          "original": null,
          "medium": null,
          "small": null
        },
        "brand_status": 1,
        "content": "Prednisolone acetate",
        "brand_info": [
          {
            "title": "Therapeutic class",
            "content": "Ophthalmic Corticosteroids"
          },
          {
            "title": "Regulatory Classification",
            "content": "Rx"
          },
          {
            "title": "Indications",
            "content": "Treatment of steroid responsive inflammation of the palpebral & bulbar conjunctiva, cornea, and anterior segment of the globe."
          },
          {
            "title": "Dose",
            "content": "Instill 1-2 drops in the affected eye(s), 2-4x a day."
          },
          {
            "title": "Contraindications",
            "content": "Hypersensitivity. Acute untreated purulent ocular infections, acute superficial herpes simplex, viral, fungal, tuberculosis of the eye."
          },
          {
            "title": "Precautions",
            "content": "Prolonged use may suppress the host immune response and increase the hazard secondary ocular infections. Use of topical corticosteroids in the presence of thin corneal or scleral tissue may lead to perforation. Remove soft contact lenses prior to administration and wait at least 15 mins after before reinserting soft contact lenses. Use of intraocular steroids. Patients with history of herpes simplex keratitis, glaucoma. Frequently check the IOP during the treatment.",
            "pregnancy_risk": null
          },
          {
            "title": "Side Effects",
            "content": "Stinging, burning, itching, irritation of the eyes, temporary cloudy vision, increased sensitivity to light, visual disturbance, allergic reactions."
          },
          {
            "title": "Drug Interactions",
            "content": "CYP3A inhibitors, cobicistat-containing products."
          },
          {
            "title": "Packaging",
            "content": "Ophthalmic solution 10 mg/mL (1.0%) x 5 mL."
          }
        ],
        "company": {
          "id": 844,
          "name": "Zuneca Incorporated"
        },

        "content": "Escitalopram oxalate",
        "brand_info": [
          {
            "title": "Therapeutic class",
            "content": "Selective Serotonin Reuptake Inhibitors (SSRIs)"
          },
          {
            "title": "Regulatory Classification",
            "content": "Rx"
          },
          {
            "title": "Indications",
            "content": "Treatment of depression, panic disorders with or without agoraphobia, generalized anxiety disorder, social anxiety disorder, and obsessive-compulsive disorder."
          },
          {
            "title": "Dose",
            "content": "<b>Depression:</b> 10 mg once daily increased after at least a week to max of 20 mg once daily. <b>Panic disorder with or without agoraphobia:</b> 5 mg once daily, increased after a week to 10 mg once daily; may be increased further up to max 20 mg daily."
          },
          {
            "title": "Contraindications",
            "content": "Hypersensitivity. Patients who have recently discontinued an SSRI and have been started on MAOI. Childn."
          },
          {
            "title": "Precautions",
            "content": "Mania, paradoxical anxiety, seizures, DM, suicide and hemorrhage. Avoid abrupt withdrawal. Elderly.",
            "pregnancy_risk": null
          },
          {
            "title": "Side Effects",
            "content": "Nausea, insomnia, somnolence, increased sweating, diarrhea, constipation, dizziness, fatigue, decreased appetite, sinusitis, decreased libido, pyrexia and yawning. Ejaculation disorder, impotence and abnormal orgasm in female."
          },
          {
            "title": "Drug Interactions",
            "content": "Ritonavir, ketoconazole, MAOIs, sumatriptan and tramadol."
          },
          {
            "title": "Packaging",
            "content": "Film-coated tablet 10 mg x 10s (P199.25)."
          }
        ],

  {
    "id": 2649,
    "detail": {
      "success": true,
      "data": {
        "id": 2649,
        "brand_name": "Zyvox",
        "brand_suffix": null,
        "brand_shot": {
          "original": null,
          "medium": null,
          "small": null
        },
        "brand_status": 1,
        "content": "Linezolid",
        "brand_info": [
          {
            "title": "Therapeutic class",
            "content": "Oxazolidinones"
          },
          {
            "title": "Regulatory Classification",
            "content": "Rx"
          },
          {
            "title": "Indications",
            "content": "Treatment of nosocomial pneumonia caused by <i>Staphylococcus aureus</i> (methicillin-susceptible and resistant isolates) or <i>Streptococcus pneumoniae</i>; community-acquired pneumonia (CAP) caused by <i>Streptococcus pneumoniae</i>, including cases with concurrent bacteremia, or <i>Staphylococcus aureus</i> (methicillin-susceptible isolates only); complicated skin and skin structure infections, including diabetic foot infections, without concomitant osteomyelitis, caused by <i>Staphylococcus aureus</i> (methicillin-susceptible and resistant isolates, <i>Streptococcus pyogens</i>, or <i>Streptococcus agalactiae</i>; uncomplicated skin and skin infections caused by Staphylococcus aureus (methicillin-susceptible isolates only) or <i>Streptococcus pyogens</i>; vancomycin-resistant <i>Enterococcus faecium</i> infections including cases with concurrent bacteremia."
          },
          {
            "title": "Dose",
            "content": "<b>Nosocomial pneumonia, CAP including concurrent bacteremia, complicated skin and skin structure infections – Adults and adolescents &gt;12 yrs:</b> 600 mg IV or oral every 12 hrs for 10-14 days. <b>Childn from birth to 11 yrs:</b> 10 mg/kg IV or oral every 8 hrs for 10-14 days. <b>Vancomycin-resistant <I>Enterococcus faecium</i> infections, including concurrent bacteremia – Adults and adolescents &gt;12yrs:</b> 600 mg IV or oral every 12 hrs for 14-28 days. <b>Childn from birth to 11 yrs:</b> 10 mg/kg IV or oral every 8 hrs for 14-28 days. <b>Uncomplicated skin and skin structure infections – Adults and adolescents &gt;12 yrs:</b> 400 mg (adults) or 600 mg (adolescents) orally every 12 hrs for 10-14 days. <b>Childn 5-11 yrs: 10 mg/kg orally every 12 hrs; &lt;5 yrs:</b> 10 mg/kg orally 8 hrs for 10-14 days."
          },
          {
            "title": "Contraindications",
            "content": "Hypersensitivity. Patients taking MAO-A and B inhibitors or within 2 weeks of taking such products; sympathomimetics, vasopressive agents, dopaminergics, SSRIs, TCAs, triptans, meperidine, bupropion, busipirone."
          },
          {
            "title": "Precautions",
            "content": "Monitor CBC in patients who received linezolid for &gt;2 weeks. Thrombocytopenia in patients with severe renal impairment whether or not on dialysis, and in patients with moderate to severe hepatic impairment, drugs that produce bone marrow suppression, and those with chronic infections. Monitor visual function in patients taking linezolid for &ge;3 months, who experience visual impairment (e.g. changes in visual acuity, color vision, blurred vision, or visual field defect). Patients with carcinoid syndrome; uncontrolled HTN, pheochromocytoma, thyrotoxicosis. Should not be with catheter-related bloodstream infection or catheter-site infection, G(-) infections. <i>C. difficile</i>-associated diarrhea. Lactic acidosis. Hypoglycemia in patients with DM receiving insulin or oral hypoglycemic agents. Monitor serum sodium levels in elderly, patients taking diuretics; risk of hyponatremia or SIADH. Patients with phenylketonuria. Increase the risk development of drug resistant bacteria. Pregnancy and lactation.",
            "pregnancy_risk": null
          },
          {
            "title": "Side Effects",
            "content": "Headache, diarrhea, nausea, vomiting, dizziness, rash, anemia, taste alteration, vaginal moniliasis, oral moniliasis, abnormal liver function tests, fungal infection, tongue discoloration, localized abdominal pain, generalized abdominal pain. <b>Childn:</b> Thrombocytopenia, loose stools, eosinophilia, pruritus at non-application site, vertigo."
          },
          {
            "title": "Drug Interactions",
            "content": "MAOIs. Adrenergic (e.g. phenylpropanolamine, pseudoephedrine, tyramine) and serotonergic drugs (e.g. dextromethorphan). Products with high tyramine content. <b>Incompatibilities:</b> Amphotericin B, chlorpromazine HCl, diazepam, pentamidine isothionate, erythromycin lactobionate, phenytoin sodium, trimethoprim-sulfamethoxazole; ceftriaxone sodium (IV)."
          },
          {
            "title": "Packaging",
            "content": "Film-coated tablet 600 mg. Solution for IV infusion 2 mg/mL x 300 mL."
          }
        ],
        "company": {
          "id": 111,
          "name": "Pfizer, Inc."
        },
        "class": "human",
        "form_class": "3",
        "prescription_class": "Rx",
        "description": null,
        "directions": null,

        "sensitivity": null,

        "generic_content": "Linezolid",
        "generic_content_ids": [
          413

        "is_prescribable": true,
        "therapeutic_class": "Oxazolidinones",
        "trusted_med_id": null,
